where \(B \geqslant 1\) bounds all mean and covariance entries and \(\varepsilon\) bounds the actual/free mean and covariance differences. The actual/short one-step difference uses exactly the same bound with \(\xi = 0\) and \(\varepsilon = H^{- F}\) from the preceding paragraph. For the coefficient polynomial of all subset moments, multiplication by \(2^n\) bounds the coefficient norm of the difference. On a ball \(|q - 1|_1, |q' - 1|_1 \leqslant M\), taking its finite logarithm costs at most \(\sum_{a = 1}^n M^{a - 1}\). These explicit bounds, and the product estimate (14.101) for calibration, are the polynomial difference bounds referred to above. All their parameters are fixed before choosing \(F\). Their powers of \(H\) can therefore be absorbed by that choice, with the required strict margins. The low-grade calibration itself is the common selected reading, not an independent choice made in this estimate.

The remaining radius and truncation tests also follow from strict power comparisons. At arity \(n\) and degree \(D\), set \(\lambda_{n, D} = 1 / [16 (nD + D + 1)]\) and reserve the reader size \(H^{\lambda_{n, D}}\). For any fixed halo \(Q\),

\(\displaystyle 16 QB (x) T (x) \leqslant H^{\lambda_{n, D}} \leqslant H, \qquad 6 \dim (\mathfrak{g}) (4 QB (x))^2 \frac{T (x)^2}{3 \sqrt{x}} \leqslant H^{- F}\)

uniformly on the half-band, eventually. The first assertion is (17.17); the second follows by taking logarithms, since its left side is \(x^{- 1 / 2 + o (1)}\). The truncation defect for a polynomial of coefficient norm \(w\), degree \(r\), entry bound \(H\) and entry error \(\varepsilon\) is at most \(wrH^r \varepsilon\), by the same product telescoping calculation. For the linear and quadratic products of transported insertions, the coefficient discrepancies are bounded respectively by

\(\displaystyle f_1 = H^{z + 2} \varepsilon, \quad f_2 = H^{2 z + 2} \varepsilon, \quad f_1 + f_2 + 2 H^{z + 1} f_1 .\)

Indeed each insertion has coefficient allowance \(H^z\), its transport cost is at most \(H\), and the difference of the two transports costs one further factor \(H \varepsilon\). Telescope a product of two such insertions to obtain the last term. Multiplying these expressions by \([2 H (2 D + 1)]^{2 D}\) and adding the truncation defect is another fixed power of \(H\) times \(\varepsilon\). Enlarge \(F\) so it is at most \(H^{- 3 / 2}\). The analogous finite partition sums at arities \(n \leqslant u\) are paid in exactly the same way using their displayed coefficient norm bounds; their degrees and numbers of terms are fixed before \(F\). Finally, with \(v = \exp (- a \text{hot} (x)^2)\),

\(\displaystyle e^{a \text{hot} (x)^2} v^2 + CH^{- 2} = v + CH^{- 2} \leqslant H^{- 1}\)

for every fixed support conversion constant \(C\) on a tail. Thus neither the Gaussian truncation tests nor the quadratic tests introduce a new analytic hypothesis after the actual reader comparison is supplied.

There are also fixed covariance-transport and station bounds. Let \(A \geqslant 1\) be the maximum of the transport multipliers of the three ordered profile pairs, and let \(C_0\) bound their fixed low-order covariance and coefficient constants. Put \(\lambda = \min_{n \leqslant u} 1 / [16 (nD + D + 1)] > 0\). Strengthen the block floor to

\(\displaystyle 2 (b + 2) \log A \leqslant \lambda \log L.\)

For the permitted comparison length \(h \leqslant \lceil (b + 2) \log H / \log L \rceil\), this implies \(2^d A^h \leqslant H^{\lambda}\) eventually: its logarithm is at most \(\lambda \log H / 2 + \log A + d \log 2\). Also \(C_0 \leqslant H^{\lambda}\) and \(C_0 \leqslant H^{1 / 4}\) eventually. These inequalities pay the finite kernel history of covariance derivatives and its two endpoint bounds, with both profiles retained; they are not a new covariance identity. If the chosen window includes the additional \(O (\log \log s)\) cover margin, its contribution to \(h \log A\) is \(o (\log H)\) and is absorbed by the same strict margin. The finite Haar station bound \(c_j\) is paid by \(c_j \leqslant \log H / (16 u)\), because \(\log H \to \infty\). Likewise every fixed profile column bound is at most \(H^{\eta}\) for its prescribed positive exponent \(\eta\). The profile-order condition \(D + 1 \leqslant p_{j - 1}\) is kept explicitly in the active grade menu below.

The remaining elementary readout inequalities use \(1 \leqslant T \leqslant H - 1\), \([2 T (2 D + 1)]^{2 D} \leqslant H\), \(C [2 T (2)] \leqslant H\) and \(C + H^{- F} \leqslant H\) for fixed \(C\); all follow from (17.17). The number of comparison steps is \(O (\log H)\), hence at most \(H\). For the quadratic coefficient allowance take \(2 H^{2 z + 2}\), which dominates \(H^{2 z + 1} + (H^{z + 1})^2\). The remaining marked finite-log difference has the already proved polynomial bound times \(H^{- F}\), and the support and gas bounds are those above. This verifies the scalar readout tests together with the fixed covariance and station bounds.

The endpoint re-expression also has the following uniform preliminary budget. Put \(\rho = H / \sqrt{s}\), \(\phi = \text{hot} (s) / \sqrt{s}\) and \(\delta = s^{- 11 / 10}\). For fixed path rate \(\sigma > 0\), path majorant and support exponent, its lost-support allowance is bounded by a fixed multiple of

\(\displaystyle W (B) \{se^{- \sigma B / 4} + H \rho^2 + s (\rho^3 + \rho^4) + \delta\} .\)

Indeed the four summands are respectively the long-path tail, the quadratic loop term (whose coefficient is at most \(2 H\)), the cubic and quartic path terms, and the inherited additive term. Their fixed support factors are absorbed in the constant. This expression is \(s^{- 1 / 2 + o (1)}\), hence at most \(H^{- A}\) for every prescribed \(A\), uniformly for physical couplings in \([s / 2, 2 s]\). Together with the already bounded forced and new-flag bounds this pays the endpoint allowances at \(H^{- 3}\), and makes their rooted sum times \(Z (B)\) less than one.

The remaining endpoint conditions are direct: \(\rho \leqslant 1\), \(2 \phi \leqslant \rho\), \(C \leqslant B\), \(B \geqslant 8\), \(\delta \leqslant 1\) and \(\text{hot} (s) \geqslant 4\) eventually. For the fixed clip relaxation constant \(c > 0\), \(\phi e^{B / c} \geqslant 2\), since \(B\) grows like \((\log s)^4\). The fixed support conversion inequalities are paid by \(B / C \to \infty\). Finally choose \(0 < a_{\text{coast}} \leqslant \text{linear} (\text{clip}) / 20\). For \(\beta \geqslant s / 2\) this gives the required tail-energy payment

\(\displaystyle 2 a_{\text{coast}} \text{hot} (s)^2 \leqslant \frac{\beta \text{linear} (\text{clip})}{5} \phi^2 .\)

These statements verify the preliminary endpoint budget for the actual re-expression, before using its marked change of weights.

Changing the marked budget at the endpoint.
We spell out the old-to-new marked support payment as well. Let the old budget radius be \(C = C (s)\), the new radius be \(B = C^2\), and let \(\theta > 0\) be the original support exponent. Fix \(e \geqslant \theta\), an old marker radius \(r_0 = s^{- a / 2}\) with \(a \geqslant 0\), a new radius \(r = H^f\) with \(f \leqslant 0\), and a desired suppression \(\Gamma = (g + 8) \log H\). Put \(u_0 = (a / 2) \log s\). Then
\(\displaystyle r \leqslant 1 = e^{u_0} r_0, \qquad 100000 C (e + 2) \leqslant B \theta / 2, \qquad 64 (u_0 + \Gamma + 100000 (e + 2)) \leqslant C \theta / 2\)

eventually. The last two follow from \(C \sim (\log s)^2\) and \(\log H = o (\log s)\). The same argument gives \(100000 C \hspace{0.17em} 2 (e + 2) \leqslant B \theta\).

Here is the resulting support inequality, rather than just the choice of its parameters. Suppose a source support \(X\) contains \(m \geqslant 2\) marked sites with pairwise lattice separation at least \(C^2\) (the old budget's squared separation test). Write \(N_C (X)\) for its connected cover number at radius \(C\). The separated-site counting bound gives \(Cm \leqslant 64 N_C (X)\), and the cover enlargement bound gives

\(\displaystyle (e + 2) N_B (X^{+ 8}) \leqslant 100000 (e + 2) + \frac{\theta}{2} N_C (X) .\)

Using \(m \geqslant 1\), the preceding three inequalities imply

\(\displaystyle mu_0 + (e + 2) N_B (X^{+ 8}) \leqslant - \Gamma + \theta N_C (X) .\)

Exponentiating therefore proves

\(\displaystyle r^m e^{(e + 2) N_B (X^{+ 8})} \leqslant e^{- \Gamma} r_0^m e^{\theta N_C (X)} .\) (17.18)

Multiplying by each nonnegative original coefficient majorant and summing at its original marked root preserves this inequality. Absolute summability is preserved by domination. Thus this is a change of weights on the actual source alphabet, not the selection of a different marked expansion. The separation test is precisely where the pair-support payment is used. In the chart application it is paid on the same chart census as follows. On a fixed compact set of distinct-point configurations, the minimum separation is a positive number \(c\). For arcs at least \(b > 0\), convergence of the tested coordinates gives lattice separation at least \((c / 2) H^b\) eventually. Since \(C^2 = o (H^{b / 2})\), this exceeds \(C^2\); the periodic no-wrap room in the chart geometry gives the same bound for periodic distance, uniformly in the permitted periods. The original marked expansion is retained in the endpoint re-expression; this change of weights does not construct that expansion. One-letter terms retain their separately paid local ticket.

We also record the resulting error bounds. Set \(y = H^{- 8}\), \(E = e^{100000 (e + 2)}\), and \(w = e^{- H^{1 / 5}}\). For a source halo \(X\) of radius \(QB\), the cover numbers of \(X\) and \(X^{+ 8}\) are at most \((2 Q + 1)^4\) and \((2 Q + 3)^4\), respectively. Consequently their support weights are bounded by fixed constants \(A_0, A_1\). Define

\(\displaystyle d_s = H^{- g - 8} + Ee^{- \theta C / 2} H^{- 8}, \qquad t_s = W (B) (3 A_1 H^{- 8} + d_s),\)
\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{rl} m_s & = 83521 e^{100000 (e + 1)} w + W (B) EH^{- 8} \sqrt{w} + W (B) H^{- g - 8},\\ b_s & = (8 B + 1)^4 e^{- a_{\text{coast}} \text{hot} (s)^2} e^{(e + 1) 9^4} . \end{array}\)

These are respectively the lost old-support bound, the enlarged rooted bound, the inherited forced bound, and the new flag-site bound. They are obtained by the marked weight change (17.18), the cover enlargement, and the endpoint re-expression; all use the original coefficient majorants. For every fixed \(A > 0\), \(Ee^{- \theta C / 2} H^{- 8} = o (H^{- A})\), \(w = o (H^{- A})\) and \(b_s = o (H^{- A})\). Also \(W (B) = H^{o (1)}\). Thus, for \(D \geqslant g + 4\),

\(\displaystyle H^{- D - 8} A_0 + d_s \leqslant H^{- g}, \qquad 2 H^{- 3} + t_s + m_s + b_s \leqslant H^{- 1}\)

on a common tail. The first follows from the strict gaps \(- D - 8 < - g\) and \(- g - 8 < - g\); the second from \(g \geqslant 6\) and \(- 8 < - 1\). Finally \(Z (B) H^{- 1} \leqslant 1\) and \(e^{H^{- 1}} - 1 \leqslant 1\) follow from the same strict power comparison. This pays the endpoint's marked budget, in addition to its unmarked re-expression, uniformly in the ambient torus.

The original mixed-grade marked expansion.
The original marked expansion used in the endpoint change of weights is supplied on the same public family, not an additional input to the chart construction. Retain \(C_R\), its transfer family \(S_R\) and its fixed interfaces \(D_R\). Proposition 4.16 supplies their common service record. For the all-modes assertion used below, retain specifically the complete finite-menu push identity of Subsection 3.16, on the actual interfaces of Theorem 13.30. Its exponential and forced gas include every coefficient of the finite census algebra. Subsection 7.8 therefore applies to that entire polynomial; no all-modes identity is inferred from an identity of singletons alone. The path and tube data are those of Proposition 13.4; the original representations and their lawfulness are those retained in Subsection 19.5.

Fix a finite census of marked slots \(V\), allowing different grades \(d_a\) and labels \(v_a\) at its slots. The census includes the prescribed signed copies and conjugates. Choose \(k < N\) with \(t_R (d_a) \leqslant k\) for all \(a\). Let \(o_i (a)\) be the migrated lattice sites and \(g_a\) their fixed motions. Require the census occupancy and separated-origin tests at \(k\), and choose \(x > 0\) such that

\(\displaystyle x \hspace{0.17em} \text{paid}_{d_a, k, N, p} (v_a) \leqslant \zeta_{d_a, k} \qquad (a \in V) .\) (17.19)

These are the original chart census and its label payment, before the endpoint change of budgets. In particular they are not inferred from an estimate on the new raw polynomial.

Let \(q_N\) be the terminal birth polynomial: its coefficient at an allowed mode \(I\) is the terminal Wilson density times \(\prod_{a \in I} O_{v_a} (o_N (a))\), with the indicated motions. Define \(q_i\) by the actual Wilson pushes from \(N\) down to \(i\). Its terminal coefficients are continuous on the compact configuration space, hence integrable; the selected transition kernels preserve slice integrability and the exact mode integrals. The terminal birth identity in Subsection 4.5 identifies this \(q_N\) with the polynomial made from the stored initial observables. Consequently its backward flow is exactly the polynomial of their original density insertions on \(C_R\).

The service proof works for these mixed grades without replacing them by one common grade. For each slot \(a\) use its own transfer sequence from Proposition 13.35, its own height bound and its own allowance \(\zeta_{d_a, i}\). All these transfer sequence families have the same background representations and interfaces. Set \(w_k (a) = x\) and \(w_{i + 1} (a) = \phi_i w_i (a)\). The adjacent affordability inequality

\(\displaystyle \phi_i L_i^{d_a} \Gamma_{i + 1} \zeta_{d_a, i} \leqslant \zeta_{d_a, i + 1}\)

propagates (17.19) separately for each slot. The positive weights, occupancy and separation propagate by the common migration argument of Subsection 4.2. Thus the per-label response construction there applies to every \(a\), with its own degree and the same transition at each step. Combine the resulting responses using the finite-census backward propagation. Its proof uses only these slotwise inequalities and the common support geometry, so it applies to this finite family of possibly different grades. At the opening level \(k\), the marched weight is again \(x\), and, since \(k < N\), the propagated support exponent is the support exponent \(\theta_k\) of the actual representation there. The resulting ordinary and forced multi-marker families have reserve one.

Here is explicitly the local form of that conclusion. There are ordinary source terms \(A_{\nu}\) and forced source terms \(K_{\lambda}\), with supports \(X_{\nu}, Y_{\lambda}\), marker inventories \(I_{\nu}, J_{\lambda}\) of cardinality at least two, and nonnegative majorants \(a_{\nu}, b_{\lambda}\), such that for every marked root \(a\)

\(\displaystyle \sum_{\nu : a \in I_{\nu}} a_{\nu} e^{\theta_k N_{B_k} (X_{\nu})} \leqslant 1, \qquad \sum_{\lambda : a \in J_{\lambda}} b_{\lambda} e^{\theta_k N_{B_k} (Y_{\lambda})} \leqslant 1.\)

The majorants include the marker tolls \(x^{|I_{\nu} |}\) and \(x^{|J_{\lambda} |}\) in the corresponding healthy value or forced coefficient norm. These are sums at the marked root, not at an arbitrary spatial site. The support terms are obtained by grouping the served ordinary and forced coefficients by their full supports and marker inventories. At fixed finite volume these address sets are finite; if there are countably many original labels at one address, sum them absolutely before grouping. The displayed majorants justify this operation and retain the reserve one.

Choose the unmarked forced presentation of the actual lawful representation at \(k\). Insert the singleton coefficients supplied by its stored calibrated fields and the grouped multi-marker terms just constructed. The ordinary and forced recombination identity of Subsection 14.1, applied coefficientwise in the finite census algebra, gives exactly \(q_k\), with the same unmarked forced gas and scalar normalization. Its singleton identity is the stored field identity; every higher mode is the corresponding coefficient of the served polynomial. Thus this is an expansion of the original marked density, not merely a bound on its integral. The endpoint re-expression retains that density and its supports; (17.18) now changes its weights with the proved suppression, on precisely these \(A_{\nu}, K_{\lambda}\). No family, interface, calibration or partition denominator has been reselected.

Paying the census hypotheses on these charts.
We verify the geometry and label payment in the preceding construction; they are not additional hypotheses on the selected family. Write \(s = \hat{\beta}_k\), \(H = \exp ((\log \log s)^2)\) and \(C = \lceil (\log s)^2 \rceil\), the original budget radius. A census slot is a pair \((c, a)\) consisting of a replication cell and one of the finitely many insertion positions. Its position is
\(\displaystyle o_k (c, a) = \omega_c z_a + t_c,\)

with the cell's fixed signed isometry \(\omega_c\), translation \(t_c\), and the chart's lattice position \(z_a\). Conjugation changes the observable, not this position. The signed copies here are the copies assigned to their cells; they are not extra slots stacked at the same site.

Within one cell, the signed isometry preserves periodic distance. On the fixed compact distinct-point test set, the separation argument above gives distance at least \(C^2\) between different \(z_a\), uniformly for the admitted arcs and periods. For different cells, let \(D\) be their lattice spacing and \(c_c\) their centers. The actual chart depth bound puts every \(o_k (c, a)\) within distance \(D / 8\) of \(c_c\), while different centers have distance at least \(D\). The paid depth surplus gives \(D \geqslant C^{10} \geqslant 2 C^2\) eventually. The triangle inequality therefore gives

\(\displaystyle \text{dist} (o_k (c, a), o_k (c', a')) \geqslant D - D / 8 - D / 8 = 3 D / 4 \geqslant C^2 .\)

These are periodic distances: the chart depth bound places each within-cell difference inside the injectivity range, and the cell centers are the actual periodic lattice embeddings. Thus the full census, including all signed copies, is injectively placed. Every site has occupancy at most one, hence at most the purchased occupancy \(r_k \geqslant 1\). Every allowed census mode inherits the same \(C^2\) separated-origin test. Migration is the exact parent/embedding identity for these cell positions, so it is the migration used by the common service.

For the label payment let \(g_a\) be the fixed requested label of grade \(d_a \leqslant d\), and let \(v_a\) be its calibrated preimage under the common chart reading \(T\). Put \(\rho = H^b\) for the chart magnification and \(\lambda = \beta_{N, p, k}^{- 1 / 2} / \theta_k\) for the original shrink factor. The exact solving equation is

\(\displaystyle \Delta (\lambda) Tv_a = \text{weight}_{\rho} \hspace{0.17em} g_a .\)

Here \(\Delta\) scales a coordinate by its colour-factor count and \(\text{weight}_{\rho}\) scales by its spatial grade. The actual coupling bound gives \(\lambda^{- 1} \leqslant 2 H\). If \(q_a\) is the largest colour-factor count in that finite frame, its coordinate formula therefore gives

\(\displaystyle |Tv_a |_1 \leqslant (2 H)^{q_a} \rho^{d_a} |g_a |_1 .\)

For large \(k\), the quantitative reading estimate gives \(\text{paid} (v_a) = |Av_a |_1 \leqslant 2 |Tv_a |_1\). Set \(S_a = q_a + 2 \leqslant d_a + 2\). Absorbing the fixed number \(2^{q_a + 1} |g_a |_1\) in one factor \(H\) proves

\(\displaystyle H \text{paid} (v_a) \leqslant H^{bd_a + S_a} .\)

The selected profile's column count satisfies \(1 \leqslant \Gamma_k \leqslant H\) by the paid column bound. Hence the actual column-weighted bound \(M_a = \Gamma_k \text{paid} (v_a)\) satisfies the same inequality \(M_a \leqslant H^{bd_a + S_a}\). The fixed panel weight \(f\) was chosen with \(f + bd_a + S_a \leqslant - 8\): indeed \(b \leqslant c\), \(d_a \leqslant d\) and \(S_a \leqslant d + 2\) give \(bd_a + S_a \leqslant cd + d + 2\), the credit already used in its choice. Thus

\(\displaystyle H^f M_a \leqslant H^{- 8} .\)

This is a payment for the original calibrated coefficient norm, not merely the healthy raw-polynomial norm. Fixed signed motions and conjugates preserve the corresponding payment; equivalently include their finite orbit in the initial finite maximum.

Let \(w_a = \deg (F_{d_a}) + 4\), so the stored grade allowance is \(\zeta_{d_a, k} = s^{- w_a}\). Choose once \(z \geqslant \max_{d' \leqslant d} 2 (\deg (F_{d'}) + 5)\) and set \(x = s^{- z / 2} > 0\). This is the generator bound through grade \(d\) used in the chart purchase. Since \(f\) is fixed, \(H^{- 8 - f} \leqslant s\) eventually. The preceding inequality gives \(M_a \leqslant s\) and consequently

\(\displaystyle x \text{paid} (v_a) \leqslant xM_a \leqslant s^{- z / 2 + 1} \leqslant s^{- w_a} = \zeta_{d_a, k} .\)

The finite maximum makes the threshold common to all requested labels, grades and panels, while the estimates were already uniform in \(N, p\) and the chart census. Finally \(k \geqslant \max_a t_R (d_a)\) holds on a tail. Thus every hypothesis of the mixed-grade marked expansion is paid on these very charts and the actual radius \(x = s^{- z / 2}\) used by their endpoint budget change.

The fixed-stage purchase interface.
Write \(p_j\) for the order of the stage-\(j\) profile and assume \(p_j \to \infty\). For each \(d\) choose three base arcs \(b_{d, i} \geqslant d + 6\), \(i = 0, 1, 2\), satisfying (14.146), with arity \(n_d = \text{trays} (d) \geqslant 2\). The explicit choice after that equation gives such arcs. Purchase the three panels initially at the larger arcs \(b_{d, i} + 1\). Apply the preceding construction to the original funded path, tube and chart data, with the common selected stamp and calibration. The local panel purchase has the following interface.

  1. The panels have one common Taylor order \(\vartheta_d \geqslant d\) and, at each stage \(j\), one common polynomial reading order \(Q_{d, j}\). Their remaining constants, including currencies, are fixed before choosing a scale schedule. If \(\vartheta_d + 1 \leqslant p_{j - 1}\), there is a block floor \(\mu_{d, i, j}\) such that the local comparison estimates apply whenever both adjacent block lengths \(L_{j - 1}, L_j\) exceed \(\mu_{d, i, j}\). The Coulomb estimates for the same panels also hold.

  2. After those lengths have been fixed, one may decrease the positive local accuracy parameter of each panel, preserving its arc, Taylor order and reading order, so that all its local capacity estimates hold for every sufficiently large coupling. This threshold is chosen at fixed \((d, i, j)\), before the background history and its finite volumes. The estimates are uniform over every reduced arc in \([0, b_{d, i} + 1]\). They supply the finite-volume legacy costs whenever the chart window has the dilation, depth and bracket of (F1), with coupling between \(3 \hat{\beta}_k / 4\) and \(\hat{\beta}_k\) throughout that window. They include the adjacent-stage estimates for each pair of stages in \(\{j - 1, j\}\) that can occur on that window.

  3. On any one resulting background history, at fixed \(d\) and common Taylor order \(\vartheta_d\), the local chart and stamp construction is valid at every sufficiently large level. Its threshold is uniform over the three panels and over reduced arcs. The stamp consists of its raw polynomial, its Wick polynomial and its linear reading, with degree and coefficient bounds and a local ticket. The representation enters this ticket only through its budget radius and healthy threshold; its halo is \(Q_{d, j}\) times that radius. The covariance and the paid label norm are those of the one selected history. These data can therefore be reused in all three panels, as checked below; independently chosen readings need not coincide.

The capacity and actuation assertions here are the conclusions of the fixed-stage construction above, applied to the named original funded data. They are not inferred from the scale-selection argument or from existence of three invertible maps. The common half-accuracy reading and calibration are those of Proposition 16.25; the quantitative quantitative bound is Lemma 17.8. The signed stamp must retain its reserved raw-polynomial and local-ticket constants on this same family.

Proposition 17.10. (One request for all grades and offsets) Use the preceding fixed-stage purchases on the common profiles, together with the common paid scale construction of Subsection 19.2. There is one enlargement of its block floors, entry thresholds and sublinear comparison windows such that every history on the resulting common calendar has the following property. Here paying the requirements includes the constant-stage comparison intervals of (19.5), not merely the block and entry inequalities. For every fixed \(d\), at every sufficiently large level \(k\), the three panels are available simultaneously at arcs \(b_{d, i} + t\) for every \(t \in [0, 1]\), with fixed weight and Taylor order. All their charts use one reading at each grade through \(d\). The background history and its requirements are chosen before any prescribed null sequence of radii.

Proof. Active grades and block lengths. Set, with the predecessor \(0 - 1\) interpreted as \(0\),

\(\displaystyle K_j = \{d \leqslant j : \vartheta_d + 1 \leqslant p_{j - 1} \}, \qquad M_j = \max (\{0\} \cup \{\mu_{d, i, j} : d \in K_j, 0 \leqslant i \leqslant 2\}) .\)

Each \(K_j\) is finite. For every fixed \(d\), the convergence \(p_j \to \infty\) shows that \(d \in K_j\) for all sufficiently large \(j\); no monotonicity of \(p_j\) is needed for this assertion. Enlarge the block floor at stage \(j\) by \(\max (M_j, M_{j + 1})\), and take a nondecreasing odd sequence above the enlarged floors, as in the common construction. If \(d \in K_j\), then \(L_j \geqslant M_j \geqslant \mu_{d, i, j}\) and \(L_{j - 1} \geqslant M_j \geqslant \mu_{d, i, j}\), including \(j = 0\) by convention. Thus one choice of block lengths pays the two adjacent lengths for every active panel. The maximum with \(M_{j + 1}\) is essential at a stage change.

Entry thresholds. Now apply item 2 of the fixed-stage purchase at these actual lengths. Denote by \(h_{d, i, j}\) a coupling threshold beyond which its conclusion holds for the resulting, trimmed panel. The trimming leaves \(\vartheta_d\) and \(Q_{d, j}\) unchanged, so the common-reading condition is retained. For inactive triples leave the accuracy parameter unchanged; all these choices are made before choosing any history. Let \(x (s) = \beta_{\ast} + s_G s\). At a fixed stage \(j\), increase the scale-entry threshold until \(x (s) \geqslant h_{d, i, j + 1}\) for every \(d \in K_{j + 1}\) and \(i = 0, 1, 2\). This is possible because \(s_G > 0\) and there are finitely many demands. Include also the finitely many local capacity and geometric thresholds described below and all the previous entry thresholds. Taking their maximum preserves every conclusion.

Additional trace and shell payments on the same calendar. The following finite-stage requirements are included before this calendar is realized. Let \(N_j\) be the nondecreasing occupancy bound of Subsection 13.4. Let \(\mathfrak{C}_j \geqslant 1\) be any fixed finite maximum of the grade-five column budgets at stages through \(j + 1\). Increase the coupling entry at stage \(j\) to include

\(\displaystyle \exp \hspace{-0.17em} \exp \hspace{-0.17em} (\max \{N_{j + 1}, L_{j + 1}, \mathfrak{C}_j, 1\}) .\)

Such an increase is allowed by (19.9). At a physical level \(m\) put \(j = J (m + 1)\). Since the stage rises by at most one per step, this gives

\(\displaystyle N_{J (m + 1)}, N_{J (m + 2)}, L_{J (m + 1)}, \mathfrak{C}_j \leqslant \log \log \hat{\beta}_m .\) (17.20)

In particular each of these quantities is at most \(\log \hat{\beta}_m\) eventually. This is a choice of the common entry thresholds, not a subsequent change to an extracted history.

For each of the finitely many active shape strengths \(\mu > 0\) at stage \(j\), further require, for every \(\beta\) beyond that entry,

\(\displaystyle \frac{\mu \exp (2 (\log \log (\beta + s_G \log L_j))^2)}{\log \beta} \geqslant j.\)

The requirement holds eventually at fixed \(j, L_j, \mu\): with \(u = \log \log \beta\), the logarithm of the ratio is \(2 u^2 - u + \log \mu + o (1)\), which tends to infinity. A finite maximum pays all active shapes, including the actual shape on the transition at that stage. Thus the estimate applies also to its possibly varying strength \(\mu_m\); since \(J (m + 1) \to \infty\), its actual flagged bound

\(\displaystyle y_m = 2 \exp \{- \mu \Delta_{G, L_j} (\hat{\beta}_m)^2 / 16\}\)

is smaller than every negative power of \(\hat{\beta}_m\) eventually.

Here is the resulting numerical transfer check. Write \(x = \hat{\beta}_m\), \(x_+ = \hat{\beta}_{m + 1}\) and \(B_m = \lceil (\log x)^2 \rceil\). By (17.20), \(L_j \leqslant \log \log x\), so \(x_+ / x \to 1\). Both adjacent occupancies are at most \(\log \log x\), and \(\textsf{S} (B) = e (2 (16 B + 9))^4 = O ((\log x)^8)\) at either adjacent radius. Thus, with \(d_m = x_+^{- 2}\),

\(\displaystyle T_m = o_m \textsf{S} (B_{m + 1}) d_m + 4 d_m = o (x_+^{- 1}), \qquad (2 B_m + 1)^4 (y_m + o_m \textsf{S} (B_m) d_m) = o (1) .\)

It follows that \(T_m + e^{T_m} T_m \leqslant x_+^{- 1}\), the latter site sum is at most \(1 / 2\), and \(\exp (y_m + o_m \textsf{S} (B_m) d_m) - 1 \leqslant 1\) eventually. The same calculation with the fine occupancy proves the adjacent version. These are precisely the coarse and fine numerical duties displayed in Subsection 17.4. They do not by themselves prove the separate finite-mask or density identities there.

One comparison window. For a fixed block length \(L > 1\), nonnegative arc \(b\), and sufficiently large \(x\), put

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{M} (x) = \exp ((\log \log x)^2), \quad C (x) = \lceil (\log x)^2 \rceil, \quad h_{L, b} (x) = \left\lceil \frac{(b + 2) \log \mathcal{M}(x)}{\log L} \right\rceil + \left\lceil \frac{10 \log C (x)}{\log L} \right\rceil .\)

Extend this as any nonnegative integer-valued function below its large-\(x\) domain. For fixed \(L, b\), \(h_{L, b} (x) = O (1 + (\log \log x)^2)\), hence \(h_{L, b} (x (s)) / s \to 0\). Indeed \(C (x) \leqslant 2 (\log x)^2\) eventually, so \(\log C (x) \leqslant \log 2 + 2 \log \log x\), and rounding adds at most two. If \(w_j^0 (s) = o (s)\) is the old stage-\(j\) window, replace it by

\(\displaystyle w_j (s) = w_j^0 (s) + \sum_{d \in K_{j + 1}} \sum_{i = 0}^2 h_{L_j, b_{d, i} + 1} (x (3 s)) .\) (17.21)

The sum is finite at fixed \(j\), so \(w_j (s) = o (s)\). No uniform bound in \(j\) is required by the common scale construction. Each summand is bounded by the whole nonnegative window, which therefore pays every active panel separately. The construction preserves the previous minimum physical length and all previous windows and entry thresholds.

For completeness, this window also protects a chart whose level has advanced beyond the paid stage-\(j\) interval. Take \(i_0 = k_{j - j_{\ast}}\), the center of the constant-stage interval (19.5), chosen with the enlarged entry threshold and window. Thus \([i_0, i_0 + w_j (s_{i_0})]\) has stage \(j\). Write \(s = s_{i_0}\), and let \(k\) be a later level with \(J (k) = j + 1\) and \(t = s_k\). Put \(H = h_{L_j, b} (x (t))\), where \(b = b_{d, i} + 1\). Increase the stage-\(j\) entry threshold beforehand so that, for every later \(t\),

\(\displaystyle h_{L_j, b} (x (t)) \log L_{j + 1} \leqslant t / 2, \qquad s_G h_{L_j, b} (x (t)) \log L_{j + 1} \leqslant x (t) / 4.\)

These inequalities are possible by the sublinear bound just proved. If \(t \leqslant 3 s\), monotonicity of \(h_{L_j, b}\) on its large-\(x\) domain gives \(H \leqslant h_{L_j, b} (x (3 s)) \leqslant w_j (s)\), and the end of the paid interval lies strictly before \(k\). Hence \(H \leqslant k - i_0\). If \(t > 3 s\), use the first of these inequalities. Since block lengths are nondecreasing and the stages from \(i_0\) to \(k\) are \(j\) or \(j + 1\), \(t - s \leqslant (k - i_0) \log L_{j + 1}\). Now \(t - s > 2 t / 3 > t / 2\), so again \(H \leqslant k - i_0\). Thus the backward depth paid by \(H\) cannot leave the two stages. Write \(d_1, d_2\) for its first and second summands. All block lengths between \(i_0\) and \(k\) are at least \(L_j\). Hence, for every reduced arc \(0 \leqslant u \leqslant b\),

\(\displaystyle \frac{a_{k - d_1}}{a_k} \geqslant L_j^{d_1} \geqslant \mathcal{M} (x (t))^{u + 2} .\)

The middle level therefore satisfies \(\ell \geqslant k - d_1\). Since \(k - i_0 \geqslant d_1 + d_2\), the level \(\ell - d_2\) is still at least \(i_0\), and

\(\displaystyle \frac{a_{\ell - d_2}}{a_{\ell}} \geqslant L_j^{d_2} \geqslant C (x (t))^{10} .\)

The root depth consequently satisfies \(w \geqslant \ell - d_2 \geqslant k - H\). This proves the dilation, depth and two-stage bracket. Since every block length on \([w, k]\) is at most \(L_{j + 1}\),

\(\displaystyle \hat{\beta}_k - \hat{\beta}_w = s_G \log (a_w / a_k) \leqslant s_G H \log L_{j + 1} \leqslant x (t) / 4.\)

Thus the second displayed entry inequality bounds the coupling decrease in both cases. This proves the \(3 / 4\) plateau required in item 2 of the fixed-stage purchase, not merely the weaker \(1 / 2\) plateau used elsewhere. Include also the eventual monitor bound \(\max (L_j, L_{j + 1}) \leqslant \mathcal{M} (x (t))\) in the entry threshold.

Offsets and common readings. Fix \(d\). Since the stage map diverges, \(d \in K_{J (k)}\) for all large \(k\). The preceding construction and item 2 of the fixed-stage purchase therefore give the legacy costs for all three panels, uniformly for every smaller nonnegative arc. Replace their arcs by \(b_{d, i} + t\), \(0 \leqslant t \leqslant 1\). Their lower bounds are preserved because \(b_{d, i} \geqslant d + 6\); their other data are unchanged. Intersect the finitely many eventual level sets for the three panels with the set supplied by item 3 of the fixed-stage purchase, and with \(k \geqslant \max_{e \leqslant d} \text{ref} (e)\). The resulting tail is independent of \(t\). Use the reading of panel \(0\) for the chart maps. For another panel retain exactly the same raw polynomial, Wick polynomial and reading. The degree and coefficient bounds are unchanged. The halo is unchanged because \(Q_{d, j}\) and the budget radius agree; the locality and healthy-set estimate are unchanged because the healthy threshold also agrees. Finally the covariance and paid label norm belong to the same history. Thus every clause of the stamp still holds, now for this panel's representation. Its individual actuation witness and this transported stamp certify the same chart. This is a reuse of one stamp, not an assertion that independently chosen stamps have equal readings. This proves simultaneous availability, rather than three unrelated chart choices. Every choice preceded the null sequence; the later interpolation and subsequence selection only query these already constructed panels.\(\Box\)

That theorem also requires a simple gauge group, the funded chart-control estimate, a bound on the generator through grade \(d\), an index \(l\) with \(l \leqslant \text{trays} (d)\), a positive sequence tending to zero, and the selected extraction order, smooth table, point limits, and reference hierarchy used by the station construction. It returns a strictly increasing level curve and, at a selected point, data \((k, i, b, c, R)\) such that the requested scale equals the exact clock gap at level \(i\) and base arc \(b\). Its chart has \(5 < b\) and \(b\) no larger than the permitted angular bound, covers all reference grades through \(d\), and supplies the finite-volume charts and invoices whenever \(i < N\). It also supplies a pairwise comparison receipt at cost \(\text{costHint} (l)\) and a station whose lift has a finite norm bound, preserves the unit, real structure, and signed action, and obeys the short-test estimate for arity at most \(l\), tests in the stated window, and error \(\text{costHint} (l)\). The proof of this quantitative upgrade is the separate three-panel chart result; this section uses that exact output and proves the scalar gap interpolation and recursion. In the chart family construction one takes \(l = d\), so the chart cutoff, arity cap, and finite grade cutoff agree. The clock gives, for each fixed base arc \(b \geqslant 0\), a positive gap

\(\displaystyle g_b (k) = a_k m_k^b,\) (17.22)

where \(a_k > 0\), \(m_k \geqslant 1\), \(g_b (k) \to 0\), and eventually \(g_b (k) \leqslant g_{b + 1} (k + 1)\). These scale-gap facts are the input to the selection argument.

Lemma 17.11. (Clock-gap interpolation) Fix \(b \geqslant 0\). Suppose \(g_b (k) > 0\), \(g_b (k) \to 0\), and \(g_b (k) \leqslant g_{b + 1} (k + 1)\) for all \(k \geqslant k_0\). For every \(J \geqslant k_0\) there is \(r > 0\) such that for each \(0 < s \leqslant r\) there are \(k > J\) and \(t \in [0, 1]\) with \(s = g_{b + t} (k)\).

Proof. Take \(r = g_b (J) > 0\). Given \(0 < s \leqslant r\), choose the least \(k > J\) with \(g_b (k) \leqslant s\); it exists by convergence to zero. Minimality gives \(s \leqslant g_b (k - 1)\), and the one-step comparison at \(k - 1\) gives

\(\displaystyle g_b (k) \leqslant s \leqslant g_b (k - 1) \leqslant g_{b + 1} (k) .\)

The function \(t \mapsto g_{b + t} (k) = a_k m_k^{b + t}\) is continuous on \([0, 1]\). The intermediate value theorem supplies the desired \(t\).\(\Box\)

17.2.2The fields of a chart witness

The producers of the pairwise receipt use several fields of the finite-volume chart witness. We list them here, with the two scale definitions they require. We then prove three lemmas: the corrected labels solve their reading equation, the insertions lie inside the home chamber at the root depth, and the clock of Subsection 19.2 supplies the dilation, depth, bracket and plateau. The lawful history (F3) is supplied by Corollary 16.29, and the readings and stamps of (F5) by Proposition 16.25, under their stated funded construction hypotheses. In this subsection, \(k\) denotes the level of the chart and \(\mu_k\) the monitor \(m_k\) of (17.22). \(J\) is the stage clock and \(L_j\) are the block lengths of Subsection 19.2, and \(c_k = \lceil (\log \hat{\beta}_k)^2 \rceil\) is the cover size of the clock at level \(k\). The monitor is \(\mu_k =\mathcal{M}_k\) of Subsection 14.19, and \(\hat{\beta}_j\) is the clock coupling of Subsection 19.2.

Two levels attached to a chart.
Let the chart have base arc \(b\), and put \(m = \mu_k^b\). Its middle level and root depth are
\(\displaystyle \ell = \max \left\{ j \leqslant k : \frac{a_j}{a_k} \geqslant m \mu_k^2 \right\}, \qquad w = \max \left\{ j \leqslant \ell : \frac{a_j}{a_{\ell}} \geqslant c_k^{10} \right\},\)

with the convention \(\max \varnothing = 0\). Thus \(w \leqslant \ell \leqslant k\). The middle cell of the chart is a cell of spacing \(a_{\ell}\). The root tests and the chamber condition of the chart are taken on the centered tiling at depth \(w\), whose cells are indexed by the sites of the level-\(w\) torus.

The witness fields.
A chart witness at level \(k\) with base arc \(b\) supplies the following fields. Here \(M\) is the marker allowance of the chart, \(N > k\) and \(p\) are arbitrary.
    (F1)

    Allowance. Four conditions hold:

    • dilation: \(m \mu_k^2 \leqslant a_{\ell} / a_k\), so the set defining \(\ell\) is nonempty;

    • depth: \(c_k^{10} \leqslant a_w / a_{\ell}\);

    • wheel: \(\min (L_{J (k)}, L_{J (k) - 1}) \geqslant 2\), where the clock factor of a stage is its block length;

    • bracket: \(J (k) - 1 \leqslant J (j)\) for every \(w \leqslant j \leqslant k\).

    (F2)

    Plateau. \(\hat{\beta}_k / 2 \leqslant \hat{\beta}_j \leqslant \hat{\beta}_k\) for every \(w \leqslant j \leqslant k\).

    (F3)

    Lawful history. For each \(N > k\) and \(p\) there are representations \(P_j\) on the level-\(j\) torus, \(w \leqslant j \leqslant k\), along the course of the chart. Each \(P_j\) satisfies the suppositions of Proposition 14.50(2) at level \(j\), with the clip and row bound of the stage \(\pi_{J (j)}\). Its budget radius is \(\lceil (\log \hat{\beta}_j)^2 \rceil^2\), and its threshold is

    \(\displaystyle \theta_{P_j} = \hat{\beta}_j^{- 1 / 2} \exp ((\log \log \hat{\beta}_j)^{3 / 2}),\)

    the clock's hatted coupling \(\hat{\beta}_j^{- 1 / 2}\) at \(j\) times the healthy allowance \(\exp ((\log \log \hat{\beta}_j)^{3 / 2})\). This is the threshold of the representation itself. Once \(\log \log \hat{\beta}_j \geqslant 1\) it is at most the clock threshold \(\theta_{\text{clock}} (j) = \hat{\beta}_j^{- 1 / 2} \mathcal{M}_j\) of Subsection 14.19.6, in which the healthy allowance is replaced by the monitor \(\mathcal{M}_j\); the numerical late-scale bounds there concern \(\theta_{\text{clock}}\).

    (F4)

    Ticket. \(T_M (\pi_{J (w)}) \leqslant \log \mu_k / (16 M)\).

    (F5)

    Readings and stamps. The level satisfies \(\text{ref} (d') \leqslant k\) for every grade \(d' \leqslant d\), where \(\text{ref}\) is the reference level of Subsection 16.8. This holds when the chart is an output of the one-step construction, which covers all reference grades through \(d\), and in any case for all large \(k\), since only finitely many grades occur. For every grade \(d' \leqslant d\) there is a linear reading \(T_{d'}\) of the grade-\(d'\) label space with three properties:

    • it fixes the reference unit;

    • it commutes with the real structure;

    • it satisfies the half-accuracy bound \(|a (v) - T_{d'} v|_1 \leqslant \frac{1}{2} |a (v) |_1\), where \(a = A_{k, N, p}\) is the calibrated entry (primary-coordinate) map at level \(k\) for \((N, p)\).

    It also carries a stamp duty with the same reading: a bound, in the halo of the readers, on the bound of the insertions whose low-grade reading is \(T_{d'}\). This clause is supplied for the funded calibrated family by Proposition 16.25; its hypotheses concern the same raw polynomial and reading, not separate choices.

By (F1), every level of \([w, k]\) lies in the stage \(J (k)\) or \(J (k) - 1\). Hence \(J (w) \geqslant J (k) - 1\), and \(w \to \infty\) as \(k \to \infty\) because \(J\) diverges.

Corrected labels.
Write \(\sigma = \sigma_{k, N, p} = (\beta_{N, p, k}^{1 / 2} \hspace{0.17em} \theta_k)^{- 1} > 0\) for the shrink factor, where \(\beta_{N, p, k}\) is the actual coupling of Subsection 16.8 and \(\theta_k = \theta_{\text{clock}} (k)\) is the clock threshold. Write \(W_{\rho}\) for the linear map multiplying each coordinate of a label by \(\rho\) to its position degree, and \(S_{\sigma}\) for multiplication by \(\sigma\) to the factor degree. Both act on the grade-\(d'\) label space \(V_{d'}\). By Lemma 17.12 below, \(T_{d'}\) is invertible. The corrected label of a basis vector \(e\) of \(V_{d'}\) is
\(\displaystyle X_{N, p} e = T_{d'}^{- 1} (S_{\sigma}^{- 1} W_m e) .\)

These are the linear maps \(X^{(i)}_{N, p}\) of input (G2) in Subsection 18.21, for the chart selected at index \(i\).

Lemma 17.12. (Corrected labels solve the reading equation) The reading \(T_{d'}\) is bijective, and \(X_{N, p} e\) is the unique label \(v\) with

\(\displaystyle S_{\sigma} (T_{d'} v) = W_m e.\) (17.23)

Proof. Since \(\text{ref} (d') \leqslant k < N\) by (F5), the calibrated entry map satisfies the reference inverse estimate (16.54) of Subsection 16.8, \(|v|_1 \leqslant D_k |a (v) |_1\). With the half-accuracy bound of (F5), the argument given there shows that \(T_{d'}\) is injective, hence bijective. Next, \(S_{\sigma}\) is invertible. So the equation has the unique solution \(X_{N, p} e\); this is (16.55) with \(\rho = m\).\(\Box\)

Status.
Fields (F1)–(F5) are fields of the funded witness. For the clock of Subsection 19.2 and all sufficiently large \(k\), with the requests of (14.135) included, (F1), (F2) and (F4) are proved below: by Lemma 17.14, the remark after it, and Proposition 14.50. The inputs listed elsewhere that these fields supply are:

  • the allowance and the root-depth identification used in Subsection 18.22;

  • the bracket property used after Proposition 14.50;

  • the ticket at the output level.

Corollary 16.29 supplies (F3) on the entire window, while Proposition 16.25 supplies (F5), under their stated funded hypotheses. These local results do not replace the common all-grade purchase proved above.

Lemma 17.13. (Chamber interiority at the root depth) Consider a chart at level \(k\) with base arc \(b\), middle level \(\ell\) and root depth \(w\). Let \(x\) be fit for the chart: the grid configuration \(z = a_k \lceil mx \rceil\) is coordinatewise separated and \(|z_{c, \alpha} | \leqslant a_{\ell} / 8\) for \(c = 0, 1\) and \(\alpha = 1, \ldots, 4\). Fix a grade \(d\), and let \(Z_d\) bound the coordinates of the finite stencil \(S_d\) of the grade-\(d\) insertions. Then, for every \(N \geqslant k\) with

\(\displaystyle a_N (Z_d + 2) < \tfrac{3}{8} \hspace{0.17em} a_w,\) (17.24)

every period \(p\) and every grade-\(d\) label, the insertion at the site \(z_c\) (\(c = 0, 1\)) has every link of its stencil, including both endpoints, strictly inside the home chamber of the centered tiling at depth \(w\). It is therefore in the chamber-local domain of that tiling. Condition (17.24) holds for all large \(N\), with a threshold depending only on \(w\) and \(d\). In particular (G4) of Subsection 18.21 holds with \(w_i = w\) for the chart selected at index \(i\).

Proof. Measure positions in mesh steps of the level-\(N\) lattice; since \(N \geqslant k\), \(a_k / a_N\) is an integer. The depth-\(w\) cell has an odd number \(q = a_w / a_N\) of steps along each axis, as in the interiority argument of Subsection 4.2. Its home cell consists of the offsets \(u\) with \(|u_{\alpha} | \leqslant (q - 1) / 2\) from the centre. Hence the site \(z_c\) has integer offset \(u = z_c / a_N\). By fitness, and because \(w \leqslant \ell\) implies \(a_{\ell} \leqslant a_w\),

\(\displaystyle |u_{\alpha} | \leqslant \frac{a_{\ell}}{8 a_N} \leqslant \frac{q}{8} .\)

A link of the stencil at \(z_c\) has endpoints \(u + s\) and \(u + s + e\), with \(s \in S_d\) and \(e\) a unit vector. Each coordinate of an endpoint is at most \(q / 8 + Z_d + 1\) in absolute value. By (17.24), \(Z_d + 2 < 3 q / 8\). Hence

\(\displaystyle \frac{q}{8} + Z_d + 1 < \frac{q}{2} - 1 < \frac{q - 1}{2},\)

so both endpoints lie strictly inside the home cell. The insertion is a bounded, measurable, gauge-invariant function of these links. It is therefore chamber-admissible, exactly as in the argument cited above. Finally, \(a_N \to 0\) while \(a_w > 0\) and \(Z_d\) are fixed, so (17.24) holds for all large \(N\). The threshold does not depend on \(p\), on the label, or on the fit configuration.\(\Box\)

Lemma 17.14. (The clock supplies the dilation, depth, bracket and plateau) Let the clock be the one of Subsection 19.2, so that \(\hat{\beta}_m = \beta_{\ast} + s_G s_m\), \(a_m = e^{- s_m}\), \(s_{m + 1} = s_m + \log L_{J (m + 1)}\), \(L_j \geqslant 3\), and \(J = j_{\ast} + J_0\). Let the monitor be \(\mu_k = \exp ((\log \log \hat{\beta}_k)^2)\) and the cover size \(c_k = \lceil (\log \hat{\beta}_k)^2 \rceil\). Fix \(\bar{b} > 0\). Then for all sufficiently large \(k\) and every base arc \(0 < b \leqslant \bar{b}\), the following hold.

  1. The sets defining \(\ell\) and \(w\) are nonempty. Hence \(m \mu_k^2 \leqslant a_{\ell} / a_k\) and \(c_k^{10} \leqslant a_w / a_{\ell}\); these are the dilation and depth of (F1).

  2. \(J (k) - 1 \leqslant J (j)\) for every \(w \leqslant j \leqslant k\); this is the bracket of (F1).

  3. \(\hat{\beta}_k / 2 \leqslant \hat{\beta}_j \leqslant \hat{\beta}_k\) for every \(w \leqslant j \leqslant k\); this is (F2).

Proof. Growth of the scale. At every step \(m\) with \(J (m + 1) > j_{\ast}\), the entry requirement (19.7) gives \(\log L_{J (m + 1)} \leqslant s_m / J (m + 1)\), so \(s_{m + 1} \leqslant 2 s_m\) at each such step. The steps of the first plateau are finitely many. Hence \(s_k \leqslant C_0 2^k\) for a constant \(C_0\), and

\(\displaystyle \log \log \hat{\beta}_k \leqslant \log k + C_1\)

for large \(k\), with a constant \(C_1\). On the other side, \(s_k \geqslant k \log 3\) by (19.6).

Item 1. The set defining \(\ell\) contains \(j = 0\) as soon as \(a_0 / a_k = e^{s_k} \geqslant m \mu_k^2 = \mu_k^{b + 2}\). Now \(e^{s_k} \geqslant 3^k\), while \(\mu_k^{b + 2} \leqslant \exp \{(\bar{b} + 2) (\log k + C_1)^2 \}\), so this holds for large \(k\). The defining inequality then holds at its maximum \(\ell\). If \(\ell < k\), maximality gives \(3^{k - \ell - 1} \leqslant a_{\ell + 1} / a_k < \mu_k^{b + 2}\), because \(a_{\ell + 1} / a_k = \prod_{t = \ell + 2}^k L_{J (t)}\) and \(L_j \geqslant 3\). Likewise, if \(w < \ell\), then \(3^{\ell - w - 1} \leqslant a_{w + 1} / a_{\ell} < c_k^{10}\), once \(w\) is defined. Hence

\(\displaystyle k - \ell \leqslant 1 + \frac{(\bar{b} + 2) (\log k + C_1)^2}{\log 3}, \qquad \ell - w \leqslant 1 + \frac{10 \log c_k}{\log 3} .\)

The first bound gives \(k - \ell = O ((\log k)^2)\), so \(\ell / k \to 1\). Since \(c_k \leqslant 2 (\log \hat{\beta}_k)^2\) for large \(k\) and \(\log \log \hat{\beta}_k \leqslant \log k + C_1\), we have \(\log c_k = O (\log k)\). Thus \(10 \log c_k = o (\ell)\), and for large \(k\), \(a_0 / a_{\ell} \geqslant 3^{\ell} \geqslant c_k^{10}\). The set defining \(w\) is therefore nonempty. Adding the two bounds gives a constant \(C_2\) with

\(\displaystyle k - w \leqslant C_2 (\log k)^2 .\)

Item 2. Let \(j = J_0 (k)\), so \(A_j \leqslant k < A_{j + 1}\) in the notation of Subsection 19.2. If \(w \geqslant A_j\), every level of \([w, k]\) has \(J_0 = j\). Otherwise \(w < A_j \leqslant k\), and so \(k - A_j \leqslant k - w \leqslant C_2 (\log k)^2\). For large \(k\) this forces \(k \leqslant 2 A_j\). By the recursion,

\(\displaystyle A_j - A_{j - 1} = b_j + 2 k_{j - 1} + 1 \geqslant 4 A_{j - 1} + 3,\)

so \(A_j - A_{j - 1} \geqslant \tfrac{4}{5} A_j\). For large \(k\), and hence large \(A_j\),

\(\displaystyle k - w \leqslant C_2 (\log 2 A_j)^2 \leqslant \tfrac{4}{5} A_j \leqslant A_j - A_{j - 1} .\)

Therefore \(w \geqslant k - (A_j - A_{j - 1}) \geqslant A_{j - 1}\), and every level of \([w, k]\) has \(J_0 \geqslant j - 1\).

Item 3. Since \(s_m\) is increasing, \(\hat{\beta}_j \leqslant \hat{\beta}_k\) for \(j \leqslant k\), and \(\hat{\beta}_j \geqslant \hat{\beta}_w\) for \(j \geqslant w\). Next, \(\hat{\beta}_k - \hat{\beta}_w = s_G \log (a_w / a_k)\). Split \(a_w / a_k\) into four factors:

\(\displaystyle \frac{a_w}{a_k} = \frac{a_w}{a_{w + 1}} \cdot \frac{a_{w + 1}}{a_{\ell}} \cdot \frac{a_{\ell}}{a_{\ell + 1}} \cdot \frac{a_{\ell + 1}}{a_k} .\)

Omit the factors whose indices coincide. By maximality the second and fourth factors are below \(c_k^{10}\) and \(\mu_k^{b + 2}\). The first and third are block lengths \(L_{J (t + 1)}\) with \(t \geqslant w\). By the entry requirement each is at most \(\exp (s_k / J (t + 1)) \leqslant \exp (s_k / (J (k) - 1))\), using item 2. Hence

\(\displaystyle \log \frac{a_w}{a_k} \leqslant \frac{2 s_k}{J (k) - 1} + 10 \log c_k + (\bar{b} + 2) (\log k + C_1)^2 .\)

Since \(J (k) \to \infty\) and \(s_k \geqslant k \log 3\), the right side is at most \(s_k / 2\) for large \(k\). Thus \(\hat{\beta}_k - \hat{\beta}_w \leqslant s_G s_k / 2 \leqslant \hat{\beta}_k / 2\), because \(\hat{\beta}_k \geqslant s_G s_k\).\(\Box\)

The wheel bound of (F1) is also immediate: the clock factor of a stage \(j\) is its block length \(L_j \geqslant 3\). Consequently, for all sufficiently large \(k\) and base arcs up to \(\bar{b}\), of the witness fields, (F3) and (F5) are supplied by the conditional constructions just cited; the remaining issue is to pay their hypotheses on the same selected history, as in Proposition 17.10. (F4) is replaced by Proposition 14.50.

17.2.3Choosing scales and chart levels

Lemma 17.15. (Strict scale selection) Assume the one-step chart producer has this output at every grade \(d\): for every previous index \(N\) and every \(\theta > 0\), it supplies an index \(n > N\), a level \(i \geqslant d\), and a grade-\(d\) chart with exact radius \(s_n < \theta\) and the finite-level receipts above. Let \(s_n > 0\) and \(s_n \to 0\). Then there are strictly increasing indices \(n_d\), levels \(i_d \geqslant d\), and admissible charts at grade \(d\) and level \(i_d\), such that

\(\displaystyle s_{n_0} < 1, \qquad s_{n_{d + 1}} < s_{n_d}, \qquad i_d \geqslant d.\)

In particular \(i_d \to \infty\) and the selected chart radius is exactly \(s_{n_d}\).

Proof. For \(d = 0\), apply the one-step output with previous index \(0\) and threshold \(1\). For \(d > 0\), apply it with previous index \(n_{d - 1}\) and threshold \(s_{n_{d - 1}}\). It gives \(n_0 > 0\), \(s_{n_0} < 1\), and thereafter \(n_d > n_{d - 1}\), \(s_{n_d} < s_{n_{d - 1}}\), with a grade-\(d\) chart at level \(i_d \geqslant d\) and exact radius \(s_{n_d}\). Induction proves the claims; \(i_d \geqslant d\) implies \(i_d \to \infty\).\(\Box\)

Proposition 17.16. (Actual charts at prescribed radii) Fix the common funded family of Proposition 17.10, with its rank and root-ticket requests included before selecting the history. Fix its extracted smooth table and one refining ultrafilter. Then every positive null sequence has the one-stage selection property (17.8). Its charts have the bounded lifts, unit, reality, signed covariance, dimension and uniform short-test receipts of Theorem 17.5. The family, table and ultrafilter do not depend on the null sequence.

Proof. Fix a finite request: grades and arities at most \(d\), a compact configuration window, and positive error \(\varepsilon\). Use all three panels of Proposition 17.10, with the same reading through grade \(d\). Their available levels contain a tail, uniformly in the common offset \(t \in [0, 1]\). The choices of exponents in (14.146) give strictly negative error exponents. Adding \(t\) to all three panel arcs leaves their differences unchanged and can only improve the terms with the negative principal arc. Thus the estimates of Subsection 14.20 are uniform in \(t\). We also check the cell widths when the window crosses a stage boundary. Write \(H =\mathcal{M}_k\) and let \(L_{\max}\) be the larger of its two adjacent block lengths. The paid monitor bound is \(L_{\max} \leqslant H\). Maximality of the middle and root levels gives

\(\displaystyle \frac{a_{\ell}}{a_k} \leqslant L_{\max} H^{b + t + 2}, \qquad \frac{a_w}{a_{\ell}} \leqslant L_{\max} c_k^{10} .\)

At large levels \(c_k^{10} \leqslant H\), so \(a_w / a_k \leqslant H^{b + t + 5}\). One predecessor step costs at most another factor \(H\), giving \(a_{w - 1} / a_k \leqslant H^{b + t + 6}\). The smaller adjacent block length tends to infinity, so \(6 ma_w < a_{w - 1}\) eventually for every arity \(m \leqslant d\). These are precisely the two cell-width estimates used for the bulk panel; the same calculation for the derivative panel gives exponent \(e + t + 5\). Thus the three-panel argument applies also across the actual two-stage window, without requiring a constant block length there. Take the maximum over the finitely many basis words of length at most \(d\). Its finite-basis extension gives, on a single level tail, error at most \(\varepsilon \|f\|_{\infty} \prod_j \|v_j \|\) for every test supported in the requested window. This does not require a common finite-volume tail over all tests: each tested upper limit passes to the same fixed ultrafilter, and the resulting bound is uniform in the test.

The finite-volume inputs here are actual inputs on this history. The joint purchase supplies the mixed-grade marked comparisons and common stamp for the panels; Proposition 16.25 supplies their reading. The whole-window lawful history is Corollary 16.29. For the additional rounded two-point comparison, Corollary 18.51 supplies the root receipt at this very chart's root depth. Its exponent and ticket choices are made in the same finite menu, before realizing the calendar. Thus neither the short-test estimate nor the rounded comparison selects a second background history.

The lift bound can be checked directly. At a fixed admitted level \(k\), the reference inverse bound and half-accuracy give \(|v|_1 \leqslant 2 D_k |T_{d'} v|_1\). Therefore the corrected lift obeys

\(\displaystyle |X_{N, p} e|_1 \leqslant 2 D_k |S_{\sigma}^{- 1} W_m e|_1 .\)

Here \(m =\mathcal{M}_k^{b + t}\) lies in a fixed compact interval at this fixed \(k\). Also \(\sigma^{- 1} = \beta_{N, p, k}^{1 / 2} \theta_k\) is uniformly bounded by the endpoint running estimate at \(k\). The two diagonal scalings have only finitely many degrees through \(d\). Their operator norms are therefore bounded independently of the terminal cutoff and period, on the admitted late set. There are finitely many grade maps, so their tuple has a finite bound in the lift space. No bound uniform in \(k\) is needed for this compactness step. The unit and real identities follow from the same identities for the reading and the two diagonal scalings. Signed equivariance follows from Lemma 17.8 and the signed stamp construction. These are identities of the chosen lifts before taking their ultralimits. The dimension bound is the quotient identity (16.93), on the same refined extraction; the free separating tests are those of Theorem 16.6.

Enlarge the level floor to include all the preceding finite requests and any prescribed lower level. Clock-gap interpolation then gives \(r > 0\) such that every \(0 < s \leqslant r\) is the exact radius of one of these charts. Given a positive null sequence and a previous index, choose a later term below \(\min (r, \theta)\), with strict inequality to \(\theta\). This is the one-stage property. Repeat with error \((d + 1)^{- 1}\) and expanding compact windows, using the strict-selection lemma. At each fixed cutoff the lift tuple has its limit along the already fixed ultrafilter; there is no new extraction at this step. This proves all the asserted receipts with their stated order of quantifiers.\(\Box\)

The one-step chart theorem obtains this output by combining the clock-gap interpolation with the funded chart family. Its quantitative comparison and station estimates are the three-panel weak-comparison result in Subsection 14.20; we use its conclusion here without duplicating those estimates.

Here is the finite-level extraction used to pass from the charts to the limiting fields. Let \(F\) be an extraction order, let \(\mathcal{U}\) be an ultrafilter on period-phase pairs finer than the fused filter of \(F\), and fix a cutoff \(D\). Suppose \(X_{N, p}\) is a tuple of linear maps, one for each grade \(0, \ldots, D\), from the corresponding reference field into the common finite-period coefficient space. Assume \(A \geqslant 0\) and, on an \(F\)-late set,

\(\displaystyle \|X_{N, p} \| \leqslant A. \) (17.25)

The finite product of these map spaces is finite-dimensional. The closed ball of radius \(A\) is compact. Compactness gives a proper subfilter limit of the bounded tuple of maps. This subfilter is finer than \(\mathcal{U}\); ultrafilter maximality identifies it with \(\mathcal{U}\). Thus the tuple converges along \(\mathcal{U}\) to \(Y = (Y_0, \ldots, Y_D)\). This is the only compactness step: it is finite-dimensional and is performed at each fixed cutoff.

If the finite-level maps preserve the real involution and signed actions on the late set, continuity of those actions shows that each \(Y_d\) preserves them. If they send the reference unit to the scalar unit eventually, the same limit identity gives \(Y_d (1) = 1\). Compose with the quotient projection to define the table map

\(\displaystyle U_d = \textsf{P}_d Y_d, \qquad 0 \leqslant d \leqslant D.\)

This order matters: the finite maps converge in the coefficient space, and the table-valued maps are obtained by applying the continuous projection after taking the limit.

For a fixed tuple of \(n\) labels of grades at most \(D\), a fixed smooth test function supported in the allowed window, and \(n\) no greater than the available arity cap, the finite-level receipt bounds the paid test error by \(\epsilon\) times the label charge and the test supremum. To pass this estimate to the quotient table, use the ordered-moment limit rather than continuity of an integral whose finite-volume measure varies. For each fixed tuple of coefficient vectors, the ordered read receipt gives convergence of the corresponding finite-volume moment to the table moment. The moment is multilinear in its \(n\) label vectors, and each label space is finite-dimensional. Expanding each moving vector in a fixed basis expresses the moment as a finite sum of products of convergent coordinates and convergent fixed-basis moments. Hence the moving-label moment converges to the table moment. This is the finite-basis multilinear limit argument; it uses the common ordered extraction and the fixed compactly supported test. The inequality passes to the limit because a closed interval is closed under limits. More explicitly, for each \(\eta > 0\) the late-set estimate is at most the claimed bound plus \(\eta\); taking the limit and then letting \(\eta \downarrow 0\) removes the slack. This proves the cutoff-level short-test estimate with no exchange of an infinite sum and a limit. It is the same moment-passage mechanism as the calibrated-smearing lemma: fixed-vector convergence is supplied by the ordered table, and finite dimensionality handles the moving vectors.

Apply this extraction separately at cutoff \(D = d\) to the chart chosen at grade \(d\) and level \(i_d\). The chart receipt supplies a bound on its finite lifts, exact reality and signed covariance, the exact unit, and a short-test error for arity \(n \leqslant d\) with coefficient \(1 / (d + 1)\). Projecting the limits gives panels \(U_d\). For every fixed finite label tuple, its grades are at most \(d\) eventually along the sequence of cutoffs; the support of a fixed compact test fits the expanding chart window eventually; and \(1 / (d + 1) \to 0\). Write \(\mathcal{T}_{r, U} (v ; t)\) for the tested integral of the tuple \(v\) under radius \(r\), panel family \(U\), and test function \(t\). Write \(\mathcal{T}_0 (v ; t)\) for the corresponding reference test value. Hence

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{T}_{s_{n_d}, U_d} (v ; t) -\mathcal{T}_0 (v ; t) \longrightarrow 0\)

for every fixed tuple \(v\) and test \(t\). This is the tested approach property. The argument is for each fixed test; it does not assert a uniform tail over all tests or all label tuples.

The final chart isomorphisms follow grade by grade. For a fixed grade \(d\), the reference tests separate its finite-dimensional field: every nonzero vector has a nonzero separated two-point evaluation. To obtain a smooth test, keep the spectator insertion fixed and choose a separated pair where the complex value is nonzero. Multiply the spectator by a unit complex phase so that the real part of the value is positive. Continuity away from the diagonal gives a small neighborhood on which that real part remains positive. Integrating against a nonnegative smooth bump of integral one, supported in that neighborhood, gives a smooth smeared test with nonzero value. Thus smooth smeared reference tests also separate. Normalize to the unit sphere. For each point of that compact sphere, continuity gives a neighborhood on which one such test has absolute value at least half its value at the point. A finite subcover gives tests \(t_1, \ldots, t_m\) and a constant \(\alpha > 0\) such that every unit vector has \(\max_j |\mathcal{T}_0 (v ; t_j) | \geqslant \alpha\). The fixed finite set of label charges and test suprema is bounded on that sphere. The tested approach estimate therefore makes all corresponding errors less than \(\alpha / 2\) for sufficiently large \(k\). If the projected grade map killed a unit vector, all its tested values would vanish, contradicting this lower bound. Thus it is injective. The dimension bound from the selected table gives the reverse dimension inequality; therefore the two dimensions agree and the map is bijective. This tail may depend on \(d\): there is no single finite index after which every grade is simultaneously bijective. For the finitely many indices before the grade-\(d\) tail, choose any isomorphism using the same dimension equality. This defines a scaling at every index. For each fixed grade, the unit, reality, and signed symmetry identities hold on its eventual isomorphism tail; the arbitrary isomorphisms used earlier need not preserve those identities. The reference test-separation input here is the all-grade free-detector result of Subsection 16.4; the separate low-grade trace calculation is not enough to justify separation in every grade. The reviewed corrected-lift argument in Subsection 17.1 provides the compatible panel maps when required by the trace family.

17.2.4Assembling the trace charts

The preceding argument proves the scale-selection, finite-cutoff extraction, tested-approach, and fixed-grade eventual-isomorphism clauses. It does not by itself prove the complete chart family statement. That handoff also needs the physical-array receipts in the selected-run transfer. For the same ultrafilter, hierarchy, and scaling these include the following. Let \(b_i > 0\) be the selected arch scale at the \(i\)th postselected level, so that \(b_i \to \infty\); let \(1\) and \(\mathcal{O}\) be the unit and the normalized curvature-square field of the reference grade-four table, and \(d_2\) the physical coefficient, as in Subsection 18.17. For a comparison table \(T\), write \(\Theta_T\) for the scalar trace of the physical stress centered by its one-point value in \(T\), and \(U_{i, 4}\) for the grade-four chart from the reference table to the selected table on its eventual isomorphism tail. The receipts are: convergence of the recentered physical stress to its prescribed principal material tensor; for every comparison table that obeys the table hierarchy laws and respects the selected table, real sequences \(d_i, e_i\) such that, eventually in \(i\), the centered scalar trace satisfies

\(\displaystyle r_i^4 U_{i, 4}^{- 1} \Theta_T = e_i \hspace{0.17em} 1 + d_i \hspace{0.17em} (4\mathcal{O}), \qquad b_i d_i \longrightarrow d_2, \qquad b_i e_i \longrightarrow 0.\)

Thus \(d_i, e_i \to 0\) themselves. The final receipt is, for every comparison table, convergence of \(b_i r_i^4 U_{i, 4}^{- 1} \Theta_T\) to \(4 d_2 \hspace{0.17em} \mathcal{O}\). The second direction \(4\mathcal{O}\) is the trace of the true-branch material tensor; the limit of the zoomed observable is a multiple of \(\mathcal{O}\), not of the unit. The arbitrary-null- sequence case needs these same receipts after the selected scaling has been constructed above. They are additional physical and field-array estimates, not consequences of finite-dimensional compactness or of the rank-one solve alone. The correction, one-dimensional kernel, stable inverse, flux normalization, and physical/reference shell comparison in Subsection 17.1 remain conditional on their displayed analytic receipts.

In the selected public construction, the one-scale chart family comes from the paid multi-scale request and its subsequent chart staging. This section proves the scale-selection and tested-chart clauses of that assembly; the physical-array, stress, and flux receipts needed for the full chart family remain separate inputs.

17.3One conserved stress tensor on the comparison curve

For lattice gauge theory precedent, see the construction of a lattice energy–momentum tensor by Caracciolo, Curci, Menotti and Pelissetto [CCMP90]. We establish the normalization used here on the particular continuum field quotient produced by the Wilson limit.

The reference stress tensor must keep its prescribed sign as the requested grades, derivative orders and configuration windows increase. We construct one physical tensor and one comparison curve with that property. The only subsequence taken below is a subsequence of the comparison parameter. The physical correlation table, and the cutoff extraction that produced it, remain fixed.

Write \(E_d\) for the reference grade-\(d\) field space and \(F_d\) for the physical one. Both have their given units, conjugations, signed coordinate actions and inclusions between grades. Their derivative maps are \(\partial^0_{d, a} : E_d \to E_{d + 1}\) and \(\partial^1_{d, a} : F_d \to F_{d + 1}\), where \(a \in \{0, 1, 2, 3\}\). Both correlation hierarchies are smooth, multilinear and faithful: a field whose separated tested correlations with every spectator word vanish is zero. Their units have expectation one and act as scalar insertions. We use the exact derivative identities for separated correlations, with derivative maps that annihilate units and respect reality and the signed coordinate action. All these spaces are finite-dimensional. A comparison curve consists of complex-linear isomorphisms \(U_{k, d} : E_d \to F_d\) and positive radii \(r_k\) strictly decreasing to zero. For each fixed grade, the maps eventually preserve the units, conjugations and signed actions; for each fixed pair of grades they eventually commute with the inclusions. These are the corrected comparison maps constructed in Subsection 17.1.

We retain the strength of their test estimate explicitly. If \(\boldsymbol{v}= (v_1, \ldots, v_n)\) is any fixed tuple of reference fields, put

\(\displaystyle K_{k, \boldsymbol{v}} (x) = S^1_{U_k \boldsymbol{v}} (r_k x) - S^0_{\boldsymbol{v}} (x) .\)

For every compact \(Q \subset \text{Conf}_n (\mathbb{R}^4)\) and \(M > 0\), the estimate is

\(\displaystyle \sup_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{c} f \in C_c^{\infty}, \text{supp} \hspace{0.17em} f \subset Q\\ \|f\|_{\infty} \leqslant M \end{array}$}}} \left| \int f (x) K_{k, \boldsymbol{v}} (x) \hspace{0.17em} dx \right| \longrightarrow 0.\) (17.26)

The kernels are smooth on configuration space. Estimate (17.26) for the completed comparison maps \(U_{k, d}\) is the transfer in the completion and uniform-test paragraph of (17.13) for the corrected maps \(C_{k, d}\) in Theorem 17.5. For the fixed finite tuple, \(U\) and \(C\) agree on one eventual grade tail independent of the test, so their suprema agree there. The short-test bound (17.12) depends on \(M\) and the labels, not on derivatives of the test, and the finite-slot expansion of the correction preserves it. Thus we use the uniform short-test receipt, not merely convergence for each individual test.

17.3.1Derivative transport and convergence on compact sets

Define the transported derivative

\(\displaystyle A_{k, d, a} = r_k U_{k, d + 1}^{- 1} \partial^1_{d, a} U_{k, d} .\) (17.27)

It converges in operator norm to \(\partial^0_{d, a}\). Here is the finite-dimensional argument, including why its argument need not be bounded in advance. Choose finitely many tested reference correlations which detect \(E_{d + 1}\), as in the separating-test argument of Subsection 17.1. Let \(L : E_{d + 1} \to \mathbb{C}^q\) be their injective measurement map and \(L_k\) the same measurements in the scaled physical table. On a finite basis, (17.26) gives \(L_k \to L\) in operator norm. Compactness of the unit sphere gives \(c > 0\) with \(\|Lv\| \geqslant c \|v\|\), and hence eventually \(\|L_k v\| \geqslant (c / 2) \|v\|\). For each fixed \(v \in E_d\), the chain rule and the derivative identity express each component of \(L_k A_{k, d, a} v\) as a correlation tested against minus the corresponding derivative of the original test. Integration by parts has no boundary term because the test has compact support inside configuration space. The approach estimate therefore gives

\(\displaystyle L_k A_{k, d, a} v \longrightarrow L \partial^0_{d, a} v.\)

Subtract \(L_k \partial^0_{d, a} v\), which has the same limit, and use the lower bound for \(L_k\). It follows that \(A_{k, d, a} v \to \partial^0_{d, a} v\). Checking a finite basis proves the claimed operator-norm convergence.

The approach estimate also holds when each label \(v_i (k)\) converges to \(v_i\). Expand the labels in fixed finite bases. The difference is a finite sum of bounded coefficients multiplying fixed-tuple errors, plus coefficients tending to zero multiplying fixed reference kernels. The same compact integral bound proves (17.26) for this difference. Differentiating one position replaces its label by \(A_{k, d, a} v_i (k)\), which converges to \(\partial^0_{d, a} v_i\). Consequently this class of differences is closed under any fixed number of position derivatives.

We give the compact convergence upgrade used here. Let \(h_k\) be any such difference, let \(\chi\) be a smooth compactly supported cutoff inside configuration space, and use the Fourier convention \(\hat{g} (\xi) = \int e^{- 2 \pi i \xi \cdot x} g (x) \hspace{0.17em} dx\). The tests \(\chi (x) e^{- 2 \pi i \xi \cdot x}\) have one common support and supremum bound. Equation (17.26) therefore yields

\(\displaystyle \sup_{\xi} | \widehat{\chi h_k} (\xi) | \longrightarrow 0.\)

For any fixed coordinate \(a\) and integer \(b \geqslant 0\), integration by parts and the product rule give

\(\displaystyle (2 \pi i \xi_a)^b \widehat{\chi h_k} (\xi) = \sum_{j = 0}^b \binom{b}{j} \widehat{(\partial_a^{b - j} \chi) (\partial_a^j h_k)} (\xi) .\)

Each term on the right tends uniformly to zero by derivative closure. In the dimension \(D = 4 n > 0\), choose \(b > D\) and a coordinate with \(| \xi_a | \geqslant | \xi | / \sqrt{D}\). Combining the finitely many coordinate bounds with the unweighted bound gives numbers \(\epsilon_k \to 0\) such that

\(\displaystyle | \widehat{\chi h_k} (\xi) | \leqslant C_b \epsilon_k (1 + | \xi |)^{- b} .\)

The right side is integrable. Fourier inversion for the smooth compactly supported function \(\chi h_k\) gives \(\| \chi h_k \|_{\infty} \to 0\). Given a compact set in configuration space, choose \(\chi = 1\) on a neighborhood of it. Repeat the argument with every coordinate derivative of a fixed order; finite dimensionality then gives convergence in the corresponding derivative norm, uniformly on the compact set. At arity zero the space of configurations is a point and the assertion is the scalar approach estimate. We have proved compact convergence of all fixed jets, including at coordinate ties that are not collisions.

17.3.2The physical conserved line

Let \(K^0\) and \(K^1\) be the real spaces of centered, symmetric, real, signed-covariant grade-four stress arrays in the reference and physical tables. Centering means that every entry has expectation zero. Let \(V^0, V^1\) be the spaces of real, signed-covariant grade-five vector arrays, without imposing a centering condition, and write

\(\displaystyle (D^i B)_a = \sum_b \partial^i_{4, b} B_{ba} \qquad (i = 0, 1)\)

for divergence. Assume the fixed reference tensor \(J^0\) belongs to \(K^0\), is nonzero and conserved, and that \(D^0\) is onto with \(\ker D^0 =\mathbb{R}J^0\). These are the reference rank and normalization requirements; the low-grade rank calculation is Subsection 16.1. The actual reference Ward normalization must be supplied for this same \(J^0\).

For all sufficiently large \(k\), the centered map

\(\displaystyle C_k B = \text{center}_1 (U_{k, 4} B) : K^0 \longrightarrow K^1\)

is an isomorphism. Its inverse is \(B \mapsto \text{center}_0 (U_{k, 4}^{- 1} B)\): the two scalar unit corrections cancel exactly, by the calculation in Subsection 17.1. Unit preservation is essential; the two expectation functionals need not agree under \(U_k\). The componentwise map \(V_k : V^0 \to V^1\) induced by \(U_{k, 5}\) is also an isomorphism. Define

\(\displaystyle B_k = V_k^{- 1} r_k D^1 C_k : K^0 \longrightarrow V^0 .\)

Derivatives annihilate scalar units, so \((B_k B)_a = \sum_b A_{k, 4, b} B_{ba}\). Thus \(B_k \to D^0\) in operator norm. Choose a linear right inverse \(R : V^0 \to K^0\) of the surjection \(D^0\). Then \(B_k R \to I\). For large \(k\), \(\|B_k R - I\| < 1 / 2\), so \(B_k R\) is injective and hence bijective in its finite-dimensional space. Therefore \(B_k\) is onto. Conjugating by \(C_k, V_k\) and dividing by \(r_k > 0\) shows that \(D^1\) is onto. The two pairs of spaces have equal dimensions, so rank–nullity gives

\(\displaystyle \dim_{\mathbb{R}} \ker D^1 = 1.\) (17.28)

17.3.3Fixing the normalization and sign once

Choose any \(0 \ne B \in \ker D^1\). In the full reference stress-array space, including scalar directions, put

\(\displaystyle b_k = U_{k, 4}^{- 1} (r_k^4 B), \qquad \xi_k = \frac{\|b_k \|}{\|J^0 \|}, \qquad \tilde{B}_k = \xi_k^{- 1} b_k .\)

Invertibility and \(r_k > 0\) imply \(\xi_k > 0\); no uniform lower bound on \(\xi_k\) is needed. We have \(\| \tilde{B}_k \| = \|J^0 \|\). Compactness of this sphere supplies a strictly increasing subsequence on which \(\tilde{B}_k \to B_{\ast}\), with \(\|B_{\ast} \| = \|J^0 \|\). Restrict \(U_k, r_k, \xi_k\) to that subsequence. All approach and eventual coherence statements persist.

The limit \(B_{\ast}\) has the reference symmetry, reality and signed covariance, since these are closed linear conditions. Its centering requires a separate check. The corresponding physical entry is

\(\displaystyle U_{k, 4} \tilde{B}_{k, ab} = \xi_k^{- 1} r_k^4 B_{ab},\)

whose expectation is exactly zero for every \(k\). Apply the moving-label compact convergence proved above at arity one and position zero. It identifies the limit of these expectations with \(\mathbb{E}_0 (B_{\ast, ab})\), proving that \(B_{\ast} \in K^0\). Moreover the exact physical conservation law gives

\(\displaystyle \sum_b A_{k, 4, b} \tilde{B}_{k, ba} = \xi_k^{- 1} r_k^5 U_{k, 5}^{- 1} \left( \sum_b \partial^1_{4, b} B_{ba} \right) = 0.\)

Operator-norm convergence of \(A_{k, 4, b}\) and convergence of the balanced arrays imply \(D^0 B_{\ast} = 0\). Thus \(B_{\ast} = cJ^0\) for some real \(c\). Equality of the nonzero norms gives \(|c| = 1\). Choose \(J = B\) if \(c = 1\) and \(J = - B\) if \(c = - 1\). This one choice gives

\(\displaystyle U_{k, 4}^{- 1} (\xi_k^{- 1} r_k^4 J_{ab}) \longrightarrow J^0_{ab} \qquad (a, b = 0, 1, 2, 3) .\) (17.29)

The physical tensor \(J\) is nonzero, centered and conserved. The reference tensor has not been rescaled or changed to accommodate later requests.

17.3.4All finite requests use tails of this curve

Fix an arity bound \(N\), a grade bound \(d\), a derivative order \(m\) and \(R \geqslant 1\). For \(n \leqslant N\), use the compact window

\(\displaystyle Q_{n, R} = \{x : |x| \leqslant R, |x_i - x_j |_{\infty} \geqslant R^{- 1} \text{ for } i \ne j\} .\)

Choose a single tail on which the exact symmetry, unit and inclusion identities hold through grade \(\max (d, 4)\). There are only finitely many grades and pairs involved. On this tail the compact convergence argument gives, for every fixed tuple of grades at most \(d\) and every \(j \leqslant m\), convergence of its \(j\)th derivative uniformly on \(Q_{n, R}\). The tolerance threshold may depend on the tuple; no uniform bound over all labels is asserted. Equation (17.29) holds on the same tail. Increasing the finite request only requires another tail of this single curve, with the same \(J, J^0\) and positive normalization sequence. This proves the coherent comparison needed before the annular Ward identities are applied. The uniform test approach, exact derivative laws and reference conserved-line certificate remain its stated inputs; the normalization argument does not supply their analytic construction.

17.4Finite-volume stress shell nodes
