This section records the actual finite-volume producer used by the stress annulus argument. It follows the selected family and its retained history. The scale choice is made before the reference opening, insertion labels, or finite list of tests. The quantitative conclusion at a fixed collar site is then obtained from a two-channel coefficient construction and the finite quota-to-norm comparison.

Write \(a_i\) for the physical spacing, \(\lambda_i = \log (a_i^{- 1})\), \(B_i\) for the support radius, and \(\hat{\beta}_i\) for the effective coupling. Put

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{rlll} r_m & = 128 B_{m + 1}^2 a_{m + 1}, & c_m & = \exp (\lambda_m / 32),\\ X_m & = \exp \sqrt{\hat{\beta}_{m + 1}}, & n_m & = \text{the number of columns at level } m. \end{array}\)

For a fixed finite-volume law, let \(\mathcal{O}_{N, q} (L, x)\) be the readout of label \(L\) at site \(x\). For an opened reference level \(p_0\), let \(\mathcal{C}_{N, q} (p_0)\) be the finite cell index set of the centered chamber tiling used by the root test, and write \(F_v\) for the prescribed translated copy of \(F\) at \(v \in \mathcal{C}_{N, q} (p_0)\). The copy includes complex conjugation exactly when the cell has odd reflection parity. Set

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{R}_{N, q, p_0} (F) = \left| \mathbb{E}_{\beta, N, q} \prod_{v \in \mathcal{C}_{N, q} (p_0)} F_v \right|^{1 / |\mathcal{C}_{N, q} (p_0) |} .\) (17.30)

This is the root-test functional of that opening, not a product over every fine-lattice site. The grade-\(D\) quota at birth level \(m\) is denoted \(\mathfrak{q}_{D, m, N, q} (z)\). It is a coefficient size in the fixed reference frame; it is not the norm in the continuum field space. The opening \(p_0\) selects the root-test chamber and its stake bound; the quota index is \(m\).

For grades \(d, D, h \in \mathbb{N}\) satisfying

\(\displaystyle D + h = d + 4,\) (17.31)

and labels \(B\) of grade \(d\) and \(T\) of grade four, define the finite fragment

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{F}_{m, x}^{N, q} (z) = r_m^h \mathcal{O}_{N, q} (T, x_{m, N, q}) \mathcal{O}_{N, q} (B, 0) -\mathcal{O}_{N, q} (z, 0), \qquad z \text{ of grade } D.\) (17.32)

Here \(x_{m, N, q}\) is the image of the fixed cover anchor \(x\) under the volume-period placement at level \(j_m\). The factor is \(r_m^h\), where \(r_m = 128 B_{m + 1}^2 a_{m + 1}\); it is not \(a_{m + 1}^h\). Identity (17.31) makes both terms have the same scaling power (the labels themselves lie in filtered grade spaces). Write \(\text{cs}_q (v)\) for the coefficient size of a grade-\(q\) label in the selected frame.

17.4.1The scale schedule and its numerical gates

Fix a simple compact gauge group, the selected Wilson family, and its same-history data. We assume the following shell-schedule input for each fixed triple \((d, D, h)\) satisfying (17.31):

\(\displaystyle t = 1 + \frac{1}{64 (d + 12)}, \qquad j_m \longrightarrow \infty, \qquad \frac{\lambda_{j_m}}{\lambda_m} \longrightarrow t.\) (17.33)

The sequence is chosen independently of the reference opening and all labels. The strict rate condition is

\(\displaystyle (d + 12) (1 - t) = - \frac{1}{64} > - \frac{1}{32} .\)

The separate lightness estimate gives, for every fixed opening \(p_0\), eventually in \(m\),

\(\displaystyle 32 r_m \leqslant a_{p_0}, \qquad a_{m + 1} (B_{m + 1}^2 + 1 + 2 \cdot 1024 B_{m + 1}^2) \leqslant a_{p_0} .\) (17.34)

The first inequality supplies the collar margin. The second supplies the number of centered chamber cells needed by the finite stencil.

Here are the channel bounds in ordinary notation. Write \(\delta_i\) for the renal allowance, \(\phi_i\) for the near allowance, and \(s_i\) for the support exponent. Set

\(\displaystyle q_i = (2 \cdot 1024 B_i + 1)^4, \qquad \text{scAllow}_{j, i} (u) = \frac{u}{16} \left( \frac{a_j}{a_i} \right)^4 .\)

The quantity \(q_i\) is the exponential-count parameter; it is distinct from the geometric radius \(1024 B_i^2\) used in the finite stencil. Let \(g_q\) be the largest factor degree among the vectors of the selected grade-\(q\) frame. This is the exponent of the threshold dilation; the mesh normalization still uses the engineering grade \(q\). The paid grade-\(q\) weight for a label-size bound \(v > 0\) is

\(\displaystyle \textsf{w}_q (v, j) = \frac{\hat{\beta}_j^{- (g_q + 4)}}{v \hspace{0.17em} C_j \prod_{\ell < j} C_{\ell + 1}},\)

where \(C_i\) is the column count. Set

\(\displaystyle A_- = a_{j_m}^d \textsf{w}_d (a, j_m), \qquad A_+ = r_m^{- h} a_{j_m}^4 \textsf{w}_4 (b, j_m) .\)

For channel weights \(A_-, A_+\), define

\(\displaystyle \rho_{m, \pm} = \frac{\delta_{m + 1}^2 \text{scAllow}_{j_m, m + 2} (\delta_{j_m} \phi_{j_m} A_{\pm})}{\exp ((s_{m + 1} + 1 / 2) q_{m + 1})}, \qquad W_m = \rho_{m, -} \rho_{m, +} .\) (17.35)

This follows from the scalar exponential weight in each channel at \(i = m + 1\): both outer and inner renal factors are \(\delta_{m + 1}\), and the input also contains the near allowance \(\phi_{j_m}\). The channel weights \(A_-\) and \(A_+\) are the paid bounds for the grade-\(d\) and grade-four insertions; the latter includes \(r_m^{- h}\), cancelled by \(r_m^h\) in the fragment. Thus \(W_m\) is a positive product fixed by the displayed formulas, not a free normalization parameter.

For a positive function \(w\) on channel names, define its marched bound from level \(k\) by

\(\displaystyle (\textsf{M}_{k, m} w) (i) = \left\{ \begin{array}{ll} w (i), & m < k,\\ w (i) \prod_{s = k}^{m - 1} \phi_s, & m \geqslant k, \end{array} \right.\)

where \(0 < \phi_s \leqslant 1\) is the selected transition allowance. These formulas are the two positive channel bounds; in particular \(W_m\) is not a free normalization parameter. The selected estimate supplies \(W_m > 0\) and

\(\displaystyle \textsf{M}_{k, m} (c_m) \leqslant \delta_m \frac{W_m}{16 X_m} .\) (17.36)

Set

\(\displaystyle e_m = 32 (1 + n_m) a_{m + 1}^D \frac{X_m}{W_m} .\) (17.37)

The second estimate, with \(\|a_k^{- D} \| = a_k^{- D}\) and \(g_{m, k} = \exp ((\lambda_m - \lambda_k) / 8)\), is

\(\displaystyle 32 (1 + n_m) c_m a_k^{- D} \frac{e_m}{g_{m, k}} \leqslant \frac{1}{2} \hat{\beta}_k^{- \nu_D}, \qquad \nu_D = g_D + 4.\) (17.38)

Here \(g_D\) is the maximal factor degree in the selected grade-\(D\) frame. The normalization on the right is the grade-\(D\) payment. For fixed \((p_0, k)\) it is independent of \(m\). To expose the rate calculation, for a positive sequence \(f_m\) write

\(\displaystyle \text{rate} (f) = \lim_{m \to \infty} \frac{\log f_m}{\lambda_m}\)

when the limit exists. The rate rules for the two channel weights give

\(\displaystyle M = (d + 12) (1 - t) = - \frac{1}{64}, \quad \text{rate} (W_m) = - D + M.\)

Explicitly, the unscaled grade-\(d\) weight has rate \(- dt\) and the unscaled grade-four weight has rate \(- 4 t + h\), since it is divided by \(r_m^h\) and \(\text{rate} (r_m) = - 1\) by the scale note (see the paragraph after Proposition 17.18). Each channel transport contributes \(4 (1 - t)\) from its fourth-power block ratio. Thus

\(\displaystyle \text{rate} (\rho_{m, -}) = - dt + 4 (1 - t), \qquad \text{rate} (\rho_{m, +}) = - 4 t + h + 4 (1 - t),\)

and their sum is \(h + 8 - (d + 12) t = - D + (d + 12) (1 - t)\), using \(D + h = d + 4\). The renal and near allowances have rate zero. The explicit bound \((s_i + 1 / 2) q_i \leqslant \sqrt{\hat{\beta}_{i + 1}}\), included in the shell-schedule input, shows that the exponential denominator has rate zero as well. In the displayed paid width, \(\log \hat{\beta}_j = o (\lambda_j)\) and \(\sum_{\ell < j} \log C_{\ell + 1} = o (\lambda_j)\), so its coupling and column factors have rate zero. Also \(\text{rate} (c_m) = 1 / 32\), \(\text{rate} (a_{m + 1}^D) = - D\) (because \(\lambda_{m + 1} / \lambda_m \to 1\), as shown in the same paragraph), and \(\text{rate} (X_m) = 0\), because \(X_m = \exp (\sqrt{\hat{\beta}_{m + 1}})\) is subexponential in \(\lambda_m\). The finite column census is likewise subexponential. The exact definition of \(e_m\) therefore gives \(\text{rate} (e_m) = - M = 1 / 64\). In particular \(c_m^{- 1} \to 0\), since \(\lambda_m \to \infty\). For fixed \(k\), \(g_{m, k} = \exp ((\lambda_m - \lambda_k) / 8)\) has rate \(1 / 8\), while \(a_k^{- D}\) and \(\hat{\beta}_k^{- \nu_D}\) are fixed positive constants. Thus the correction-bound left side has rate

\(\displaystyle \frac{1}{32} - M - \frac{1}{8} = - \frac{5}{64} < 0 ;\)

it tends to zero and is eventually below the displayed grade-\(D\) payment.

Lemma 17.17. (Paid-delay growth of the shell weights) Use the same selected history, block lengths and entrance delays as in Sections 13.4 and 19.2. In particular, its final entry thresholds retain the fixed-section delay of Subsection 13.6. Write \(J (i)\) for its stage clock and \(\ell_j\) for the block side fixed at stage \(j\), so that

\(\displaystyle J (i) \leqslant J (i + 1) \leqslant J (i) + 1, \qquad L_i = \ell_{J (i + 1)} = a_i / a_{i + 1}, \qquad \hat{\beta}_{i + 1} = \hat{\beta}_i + s_G \log L_i, \quad s_G > 0.\)

At step \(i\), assume the history pays the retained entrance threshold of stage \(J (i + 1)\) with \(\hat{\beta}_i\). Use the public weights

\(\displaystyle \phi_i = \hat{\beta}_{i + 1}^{- 1}, \qquad \upsilon_i = \hat{\beta}_i^{- \nu_D}, \qquad \nu_D = g_D + 4,\)

where \(g_D\) is the maximal factor degree of the same selected grade-\(D\) frame. Assume the retained reference depths satisfy \(D + 3 \leqslant J (\text{ref} (D))\) for every \(D\). Then, for every \(D \in \mathbb{N}\) and every \(i \geqslant \text{ref} (D)\),

\(\displaystyle \phi_i L_i^{D + 1} \upsilon_i \leqslant \upsilon_{i + 1} .\) (17.39)

This conclusion uses no cutoff or period parameter.

Proof. The delay calibration in Subsection 13.6 is made after the stage block sides are fixed. For each stage \(j\), it tests the two possible profiles \(k = j\) and \(k = j - 1\) (only \(k = 0\) when \(j = 0\)), and all grades \(0 \leqslant d \leqslant k\). Its numerical proof uses, for \(x = \hat{\beta}_i\), \(b = s_G \log \ell_j\) and \(y = x + b\), the sufficient bounds

\(\displaystyle x \geqslant 1, \qquad x \geqslant b, \qquad y \geqslant \max \{1, 2^{g_d + 4} \ell_j^{d + 1} \} .\)

These are thresholds for each fixed pair \((j, d)\), not one threshold independent of the block side or grade. The cited proof takes a finite maximum at each stage, includes the profile-cost and credit thresholds, and then takes prefix maxima together with the prior entrance requests. It proves the tail comparison for every coupling beyond the resulting threshold. Sections 13.4 and 19.2 retain this delay before constructing the history; enlarging an entry threshold preserves the comparison. Thus no new history is selected for the present application.

Now fix \(D\) and \(i \geqslant \text{ref} (D)\). Monotonicity of \(J\) gives \(D \leqslant D + 3 \leqslant J (\text{ref} (D)) \leqslant J (i)\). The pair \((j, k) = (J (i + 1), J (i))\) is one of the tested adjacent pairs, and the assumed payment permits evaluation at \(x = \hat{\beta}_i\). The block and coupling identities in the statement identify its \(\ell_j\) and \(y\) with \(L_i\) and \(\hat{\beta}_{i + 1}\). Finally the reciprocal tail \(\textsf{f}_D\) in (13.5) has exponent \(g_D + 4\), hence its values at these two couplings are exactly \(\upsilon_i\) and \(\upsilon_{i + 1}\). That equation, with \(d = D\), is the asserted inequality.\(\Box\)

For the marched estimate use Lemma 17.17. Since \(\hat{\beta}_i \geqslant 1\), its weights satisfy \(0 < \upsilon_i \leqslant 1\) and \(0 < \phi_i \leqslant 1\). The retained delay and the reference-depth condition are conditions on the same history used by the menu and transfer inputs of Proposition 17.18. The service theorem alone does not supply them; the named delay purchase above supplies the growth bound when those conditions hold. Merely knowing \(\hat{\beta}_i \to \infty\) would not imply it for varying \(L_i\). For \(m \geqslant k \geqslant \text{ref} (D)\), multiplying it for \(k \leqslant i < m\) gives \(\prod_{i = k}^{m - 1} \phi_i \leqslant (\upsilon_m / \upsilon_k) (a_m / a_k)^{D + 1}\). Together with \(\upsilon_m \leqslant 1\) it gives

\(\displaystyle \textsf{M}_{k, m} (c_m) \leqslant \frac{c_m}{\upsilon_k} \left( \frac{a_m}{a_k} \right)^{D + 1}, \qquad k \geqslant \text{ref} (D) .\)

For fixed \(k\), the right side has rate \(1 / 32 - (D + 1)\), while \(\delta_m W_m / (16 X_m)\) has rate \(- D + M\). Their rate difference is \(1 / 32 - 1 - M = - 61 / 64 < 0\), so the former is eventually smaller. This uses the common-trajectory growth estimate, the rate-zero allowance, and the paid channel-rate identity above. The separate strict condition \(M > - 1 / 32\) is a sufficient condition for the correction bound to be eventual. Hence both bound inequalities hold for each fixed opening.

The other bound gate used in the finite coefficient construction is the exact budget identity

\(\displaystyle 4 (1 + n_m) \hspace{0.17em} 2 \left( a_{m + 1}^D X_m \frac{4}{W_m} \right) = e_m .\) (17.40)

Thus the available coefficient budget is precisely the finite-census bound of the principal term, with its two channel factors; no extra polynomial is hidden in this equality.

17.4.2The coefficient and density ledger

Set

\(\displaystyle a_m^{\ast} = \frac{4}{W_m}, \qquad I_m = \frac{W_m}{16 X_m}, \qquad p'_m = a_{m + 1}^D (X_m a_m^{\ast}), \qquad e_m = 8 (1 + n_m) p'_m .\) (17.41)

Then \(e_m = 32 (1 + n_m) a_{m + 1}^D X_m / W_m\), as above. This reparameterization exhibits the numerical inputs to the coefficient construction. Put \(L_m = a_m / a_{m + 1}\) and \(A_m = L_m^D\), the block dilation of a grade-\(D\) coefficient. The block side \(L_m \geqslant 2\) and grade \(D \geqslant 0\) give \(\|A_m^{- 1} \| = L_m^{- D} \leqslant 1\). The stretch across this step satisfies

\(\displaystyle a_m^D L_m^{- D} = a_{m + 1}^D,\)

so the coefficient bound \(X_m a_m^{\ast}\) delivered by the finite transfer construction becomes exactly \(p'_m = a_{m + 1}^D (X_m a_m^{\ast})\) after this grade-\(D\) rescaling. Together with \(X_m \geqslant 1\),

\(\displaystyle 2 I_m (2 a_m^{\ast}) = X_m^{- 1} \leqslant 1, \qquad I_m \|A_m^{- 1} \| (X_m a_m^{\ast}) = \frac{\|A_m^{- 1} \|}{4} \leqslant 1.\)

The marched bound says \(J_m := \textsf{M}_{k, m} (c_m) \leqslant \delta_m I_m\). At each name the selected menu has the corresponding regular bound \(J_m\) and allowance \(\delta_m I_m\); multiplying the finite factors and using \(1 + v \leqslant e^v\) for \(v \geqslant 0\) yields the finite transfer bound with its exponential penalty. This is the finite-product estimate used by the transition, not a claim that normalized root expectations multiply.

The fine response is first built for each of the two insertions from the same terminal family, interface, and funded path. Its quiet coefficient is the background activity and its singleton coefficient is the paid target plus the hook correction; signed covariance transports these identities from the distinguished name to the other names. Backward transport uses \(\text{scAllow}_{j, i} (u) = u (a_j / a_i)^4 / 16\). If \(L_i = a_i / a_{i + 1} \geqslant 2\), then

\(\displaystyle \text{scAllow}_{j, i + 1} (u) = L_i^4 \text{scAllow}_{j, i} (u), \qquad 6 L_i \text{scAllow}_{j, i} (u) \leqslant \text{scAllow}_{j, i + 1} (u),\)

because \(L_i^3 \geqslant 8\). Iteration moves the response to level \(m + 1\); injective migration preserves occupancy and the block map expands separation by at least \(L_i\). The fine response uses occupancy \(o_{m + 1} = r (m + 1)\).

The two channels refer to the two inserted labels. Their graft is formed at the same base name; the assumed affine covariance of the graft determines its quiet and singleton coefficients. This covariance is part of the common-trajectory menu input, not a consequence of the scalar estimates. The higher many-name terms are pruned with the finite-mask receipt. With fine occupancy \(o_{m + 1}\) its mask cost is

\(\displaystyle \sigma_m = 2 (1 + o_{m + 1}) \eta_{m + 1} (o_{m + 1} e^{s_{m + 1} + 1 / 2} + 1) + (1 + o_{m + 1}) \chi_{m + 1} .\)

The selected finite-mask receipt gives \((2 B_{m + 1} + 1)^4 \sigma_m \leqslant 1 / 2\) and \(e^{\sigma_m} - 1 \leqslant 1\). The radius \(1024 B_{m + 1}^2\) in the stencil and the exponent count \(q_{m + 1}\) in the channel bound are distinct quantities.

The finite transfer step and transition then yield a coefficient \(Q_m\) at level \(m\). Its principal coefficient is bounded by \(p'_m\). The census cost is exactly

\(\displaystyle 4 (1 + n_m) \hspace{0.17em} 2 p'_m = e_m,\)

where \(n_m\) is the column count; the factor \(4\) is the two-channel and sign count. The correction has the purchased one-step remainder bound, purchased with the same parameter \(p'_m\); so the principal coefficient size plus the remainder bound is at most \(2 p'_m\), which is the quantity in the census identity. No response coefficient is declared exact before that paid correction is included.

Here is the exact density identity retained by the construction. The paid representation supplies regular coefficients \(H_S (U)\) indexed by supports \(S\) and forced coefficients \(K_P (U)\) indexed by boxes \(P\). Each regular coefficient is supported on its assigned \(S\), and each forced coefficient on its assigned box support. Write \(E_m (U)\) for the action polynomial, \(\Phi_i (U)\) for singleton field coefficients, \(\bar{A}_P (U)\) for the background activity, and \(\omega_{i, P} (U)\) for singleton activities. The operator \(\text{send}_i (z)\) is the polynomial monomial with coefficient \(z\) in insertion slot \(i\); \(F (U)\) is the target flag set. Then the pushed density is

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{rl} \mathscr{D}_m (U) = & e^{E_m (U)} \exp_{\text{nil}} \hspace{-0.17em} \left( \sum_i \text{send}_i (\Phi_i (U)) + \sum_S H_S (U) \right) \hspace{3cm} \text{(17.42)} \\ & \quad \times \sum_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{array}{c} \mathcal{P} \text{ a family of boxes} :\\ P \cap P' = \varnothing (P \ne P'), \quad \bigcup_{P \in \mathcal{P}} Q_P = F (U) \end{array}$}}} \prod_{P \in \mathcal{P}} \left( \bar{A}_P (U) + \sum_i \text{send}_i (\omega_{i, P} (U)) + K_P (U) \right) . \hspace{3cm} \text{(17.43)} \end{array}\)

Each box is \((P, Q_P)\) with \(Q_P \subseteq P\); compatibility means disjointness of full supports, while the flags cover \(F (U)\). Here \(\exp_{\text{nil}} (Z) = \sum_{r = 0}^{|I|} Z^r / r!\) for the finite set \(I\) of insertion names. The degree conditions make every \(H_S\) and \(K_P\) vanish in degrees zero and one; their regular-support and forced-box coefficient costs have separate summable bounds. Thus the higher-degree exponential terms, forced terms, and compatible box products remain explicitly present and paid. In particular, the pushed density is not identified with a single leading coefficient. The canonical local-transfer proof pays these two arrays separately and proves the same-law density equality used here.

The served transfer uses coarse occupancy \(o_m = r (m)\); the fine response and graft use \(o_{m + 1} = r (m + 1)\). Set \(d_m = \hat{\beta}_{m + 1}^{- 2}\), \(u_m = \hat{\beta}_{m + 1}^{- 1}\), and \(y_m\) equal to the flagged response bound. For the explicit rooted-site cost, put \(\textsf{S} (B) = e (2 (16 B + 9))^4\). The transfer duty is

\(\displaystyle 0 < d_m \leqslant 1, \qquad T_m = o_m \textsf{S} (B_{m + 1}) d_m + 4 d_m, \qquad T_m + e^{T_m} T_m \leqslant u_m,\)

together with

\(\displaystyle (2 B_m + 1)^4 (y_m + o_m \textsf{S} (B_m) d_m) \leqslant \tfrac{1}{2}, \qquad e^{y_m + o_m \textsf{S} (B_m) d_m} - 1 \leqslant 1.\)

Here \(y_m = 2 \exp (- \mu_m \Delta_{G, L_m} (\hat{\beta}_m)^2 / 16)\), where \(\mu_m, \Delta_{G, L_m}\) are the selected shape strength and fine monitor. These are the exact coarse transfer inequalities; the fine backward transport and graft instead use \(o_{m + 1}\). The endpoint support exponents are at least \(100\). These displayed inequalities are the paid-transfer duties on the same adjacent transition and history. Sections 3.16 and 7.8 give the conditional service and transfer arguments; the actual numerical conditions are verified by (17.20) and the estimates following it. This keeps the regular and forced coefficient charges separate and does not assume equality of the interacting and reference actions.

The actual fine finite-mask payment.
The finite-mask cost in the shell construction is the multiple-insertion cost already paid in Subsection 9.2. Here is the index and parameter identification. At the fine shell level \(k = m + 1\), use that producer on the actual adjacent transition with coarse clock \(x = \hat{\beta}_k\) and fine clock \(H = \hat{\beta}_{k + 1}\). Its occupancy \(N\) is \(o_k\), its outgoing support exponent \(b\) is \(s_k\), its ordinary allowance \(\eta\) is \(\eta_k = H^{- 2}\), and its forced allowance \(\tau (x)\) is \(\chi_k\), the actual forced bound of the two absorptions in the retained marked transition. This \(\eta_k\) is the ordinary finite-mask allowance, not the separately named marker bound \(\tfrac{1}{4} \hat{\beta}_k^{- 1 / 2}\) used by the centered service. The background transition and its forced array are unchanged: the projection-payment producer defines the singleton map by differentiating that array, and Subsection 19.4 uses that same map for its multiple-insertion estimate.

With \(\alpha = s_k + 1 / 2\), the producer's scalar \(s (x)\) is exactly

\(\displaystyle s (x) = 2 (1 + o_k) \eta_k (o_k e^{s_k + 1 / 2} + 1) + (1 + o_k) \chi_k = \sigma_m .\)

Its coarse radius is \(B (x) = B_k = B_{m + 1}\). Therefore (9.15) gives, on the actual fine transition,

\(\displaystyle (2 B_{m + 1} + 1)^4 \sigma_m \leqslant \tfrac{1}{2}, \qquad e^{\sigma_m} - 1 \leqslant 1.\)

These are the two finite-mask inequalities needed in the shell proof. For clarity, their numerical proof does not assume a uniform bound on all support exponents or on all occupancy stages. Fix a permitted stage pair first; then \(o_k\), \(s_k\), the block length and the positive suppression constant are fixed stage data. The forced bound has every negative upper exponent, while \(H^{- 2}\) has exponent \(- 2\) and \(B (x)\) has exponent zero. Consequently \((2 B (x) + 1)^4 s (x) \to 0\) and \(s (x) \to 0\), so the two inequalities hold above a finite entrance threshold for that pair. Take the maximum over the finitely many permitted pairs and degrees at each stage, exactly as done after (9.15). The common scale selection retains that maximum before realizing the history. At level \(k = m + 1\) its entry inequality applies to the corresponding actual pair, with that pair's actual suppression constant. Thus this is an already purchased same-history instance, not an inference from pointwise decay with an unbounded stage index or a new mask assumption.

17.4.3From the ledger to one collar node

Fix an opened reference level \(p_0\) and a level \(k\) with that opening, \(k\) beyond the grade-\(D\) threshold, and labels \(B, T\). The quota estimate at birth level \(m\) gives a finite constant \(Q_m^{\text{quota}}\) independent of volume and period. It may depend on \(m\); no uniform birth-level bound is asserted:

\(\displaystyle \|z\|_D \leqslant Q_m^{\text{quota}} \mathfrak{q}_{D, m, N, q} (z) .\) (17.44)

Apply the ledger and the common-trajectory coefficient-to-label transfer at a fixed collar anchor. That transfer takes a coefficient whose size plus remainder bound is at most \(c\), with census cost \(4 (1 + n_m) c \leqslant e\), to a grade-\(D\) label \(z\) with \(\mathfrak{q}_{D, m, N, q} (z) \leqslant 2 e\); the factor \(2\) is produced by this transfer step. With \(c = 2 p'_m\) and \(e = e_m\), by the coefficient budget (17.40), this gives \(\mathfrak{q}_{D, m, N, q} (z) \leqslant 2 e_m\), hence

\(\displaystyle \|z\|_D \leqslant 2 Q_m^{\text{quota}} e_m .\)

The transferred label satisfies the exact product-minus-correction identity (17.32). Its chamber condition follows from the two input chamber memberships and closure under products and subtraction of a paid correction. Write \(\Theta_k\) for the selected treasury at level \(k\). The response estimate together with (17.38) gives

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{R}_{N, q, p_0} (\mathcal{F}_{m, x}^{N, q} (z)) \leqslant 2 S (p_0, k) c_m^{- 1}, \qquad S (p_0, k) = \exp \hspace{-0.17em} ((a_{p_0} / a_k)^4 \Theta_k + 5) .\) (17.45)

To see the factor \(2\), write the pre-correction function as \(f\) and the finite transfer correction as \(r'\). The exact decomposition is

\(\displaystyle f -\mathcal{O}_{N, q} (B, 0) = (f + r') + (- r' -\mathcal{O}_{N, q} (B, 0)) .\)

Put \(B_m (p_0, k) = S (p_0, k) c_m^{- 1}\). The two root-test bounds are respectively at most \(B_m (p_0, k)\) and \(B_m (p_0, k)\) (the second is first obtained with the stronger bound \(B_m (p_0, k) / 2\)). The root-test triangle inequality applies because both terms are in the centered chamber. Thus the factor \(2\) comes from this two-term triangle inequality; it is not obtained by multiplying normalized root functionals or by combining regular and forced charges. The estimate uses the served coefficient identity, the throw transfer record, the fixed opening, and the two inequalities (17.36) and (17.38).

The birth-level threshold for the chamber receipt is uniform over all anchors in the level-\(m\) collar. The placement event in the extraction filter is instead pointwise in the cover anchor. For fixed \(x\), the period eventually places it at its intended grid site, so the radial bounds carry over to the finite-volume location. The first inequality in (17.34) is the shell-smallness condition used with those bounds to keep the remote insertion in the centered chamber; the second gives enough centered chamber cells for the finite response stencil. At each \((N, q)\) on that anchor's late set, the construction just described gives its correction. Outside that late set choose the zero label. The numerical bounds do not depend on \(x\), although the late set may.

Proposition 17.18. (Selected-history shell-node output) Take the selected public history of Theorem 4.15. Use its adjacent-scale paid menu, tariff, and served-transfer receipts from Sections 3.13, 3.12.4, 3.16, and 7.8, the scale and bound gates displayed above, the lightness estimate (17.34), the finite-stencil room estimate, and the quota estimate (17.44) at each birth level. Assume also the common-trajectory chamber receipt in the following uniform eventual form: for each fixed opened pair \((p_0, k)\), labels \(B, T\) and extraction order \(\mathfrak{f}\), there is \(m_{\text{ch}}\) such that, for every \(m \geqslant m_{\text{ch}}\) and every cover anchor \(x\) whose level-\(j_m\) location lies in \(\mathcal{C}_{r_m}\), for \(\mathfrak{f}\)-almost every \((N, q)\) the readouts \(\mathcal{O}_{N, q} (B, 0)\), \(\mathcal{O}_{N, q} (T, x_{m, N, q})\) and \(\mathcal{O}_{N, q} (z, 0)\), for every grade-\(D\) label \(z\), lie in the centered chamber of the opening \(p_0\). The threshold \(m_{\text{ch}}\) is independent of \(x\); the late event in \((N, q)\) may depend on \(m\) and \(x\), but is common to all the grade-\(D\) labels \(z\).

Fix grades with \(D + h = d + 4\). Then there are \(t > 1\) and levels \(j_m\), chosen before any opening, label or extraction order, with \(j_m \to \infty\) and \(\lambda_{j_m} / \lambda_m \to t\), such that the following holds. For every reference level \(p_0\) opened at a level \(k \geqslant \text{ref} (D)\), every \(B \in E_d\), every \(T \in E_4\) and every extraction order \(\mathfrak{f}\), there is \(m_0\) such that for every \(m \geqslant m_0\) and every cover anchor \(x\) whose level-\(j_m\) location lies in the collar \(\mathcal{C}_{r_m}\) there is a label sequence \(z_x : \mathbb{N} \times \mathbb{N} \to E_D\) for which \(\mathfrak{f}\)-almost every \((N, q)\) has \(p_0 \leqslant N\), \(\mathcal{F}_{m, x}^{N, q} (z_x (N, q))\) in the centered chamber of \(p_0\), and

\(\displaystyle \|z_x (N, q)\|_D \leqslant 2 Q_m^{\text{quota}} e_m, \qquad \mathcal{R}_{N, q, p_0} (\mathcal{F}_{m, x}^{N, q} (z_x (N, q))) \leqslant 2 S (p_0, k) c_m^{- 1} .\)

The late event may depend on \(x\); the displayed numeric bounds do not. This is the conclusion required by Definition 17.19, apart from the scale note, which is proved after the proof.

Proof. Choose \(t\) and \(j_m\) by the rate schedule (17.33); this choice uses only \(d\), so it precedes the opening, the labels and the extraction order. Now fix an opened pair \((p_0, k)\) with \(k \geqslant \text{ref} (D)\), labels \(B, T\) and an extraction order \(\mathfrak{f}\), and put \(a = \max (1, \text{cs}_d (B))\), \(b = \max (1, \text{cs}_4 (T))\). The following conditions on \(m\) each hold for all sufficiently large \(m\), and we take \(m_0\) beyond all of them: the two rate-based bound gates (by the strict inequality \((d + 12) (1 - t) > - 1 / 32\), for the fixed coefficient bounds \(a, b\)); the two lightness inequalities (17.34) for this \(p_0\); \(m \geqslant k\), so that \(p_0 \leqslant k \leqslant m\) and \(p_0\) is opened at \(k\); \(j_m \geqslant \text{ref} (d)\) and \(j_m \geqslant \text{ref} (4)\), because \(j_m \to \infty\); \(j_m > m + 1\), because \(\lambda\) is increasing, \(\lambda_{j_m} / \lambda_m \to t > 1\) and \(\lambda_{m + 1} / \lambda_m \to 1\) (see the scale note below); and the finite-stencil room threshold for \(p_0\); and the uniform chamber threshold \(m_{\text{ch}}\) in the hypothesis. This last threshold is chosen before the anchor: finiteness of each individual collar would not suffice to choose it for the growing family of collars. Fix \(m \geqslant m_0\) and a cover anchor \(x\) in the collar. Choose the late volume-period event of \(\mathfrak{f}\) on which \(N > j_m\) (so \(p_0 \leqslant k \leqslant m < j_m < N\)), the image of \(x\) is its intended grid site, and the three readouts of the chamber receipt lie in the centered chamber. The intersection of these finitely many late events is late. On it, the construction of this subsection gives the correction \(z_x (N, q)\): the ledger and coefficient-to-label transfer give the product-minus-correction identity (17.32), the fragment lies in the centered chamber by closure of the chamber under products and subtraction of a paid correction, the quota estimate gives \(\|z_x (N, q)\|_D \leqslant 2 Q_m^{\text{quota}} e_m\), and (17.45) gives the root bound. Off the late event put \(z_x (N, q) = 0\). The constants \(Q_m^{\text{quota}}\), \(e_m\), \(S (p_0, k)\) and \(c_m\) do not depend on \(x\). This produces exactly the pointwise late-set quantifier stated above; it does not claim one late event simultaneously for the growing collar.\(\Box\)

The scale note.
Definition 17.19 also requires \(\log r_m / \lambda_m \to - 1\). This is not part of the shell construction; it follows from two schedule properties of the selected history: the schedule is gentle, \(\log L_m / \lambda_m \to 0\) for the adjacent ratios \(L_m = a_m / a_{m + 1}\), and the radii grow subexponentially in the scale, \(\log B_m / \lambda_m \to 0\). Indeed \(\lambda_{m + 1} = \lambda_m + \log L_m\), so \(\lambda_{m + 1} / \lambda_m \to 1\), and
\(\displaystyle \frac{\log r_m}{\lambda_m} = \frac{\log 128}{\lambda_m} + 2 \hspace{0.17em} \frac{\log B_{m + 1}}{\lambda_{m + 1}} \cdot \frac{\lambda_{m + 1}}{\lambda_m} - \frac{\lambda_{m + 1}}{\lambda_m} \longrightarrow 0 + 0 - 1 = - 1.\)

In particular \(r_m \to 0\).

Shell schedule, chamber placement and birth quota on the selected family.
We verify three of the actual shell-node hypotheses without changing the history or its extraction. Write \(s_m = \log (a_m^{- 1})\). The selected schedule has \(s_m \to \infty\) and
\(\displaystyle 0 \leqslant \frac{s_{m + 1} - s_m}{s_m} = \frac{\log L_{J (m + 1)}}{s_m} \leqslant \frac{1}{J (m + 1)} \longrightarrow 0\)

by (19.7). For the fixed shell grades put \(t = 1 + [64 (d + 12)]^{- 1}\) and choose \(j_m\) as the first index with \(s_{j_m} \geqslant ts_m\). The preceding index satisfies \(s_{j_m - 1} < ts_m\); the displayed adjacent-increment estimate gives \(s_{j_m} / s_{j_m - 1} \to 1\). Thus \(s_{j_m} / s_m \to t\), proving the shell schedule with a choice independent of labels and openings.

The selected covering radius is \(B_m = \lceil \log^2 \hat{\beta}_m \rceil\), where \(\hat{\beta}_m = \beta_{\ast} + s_G s_m\). Hence \(\log B_m = o (s_m)\) and \(B_{m + 1} / B_m \to 1\). In particular \(B_{m + 1}^2 \leqslant 2 B_m^2\) on a tail and \(a_{m + 1} B_{m + 1}^2 \to 0\). Both inequalities in (17.34) follow for every fixed \(p_0\). The opening argument only needs these tab inequalities on a tail: starting at a level beyond that tail, \(a_{j + 1} B_{j + 1}^2 \leqslant (2 / 3) a_j B_j^2\) since \(L_j \geqslant 3\). Consequently every fixed reference level is opened at all sufficiently large later levels, including levels beyond any finite list of watch depths. No alteration of an already chosen reference level is needed.

Fix \(p_0\) and grades \(d, 4, D\). The actual local sections have finite stencils depending on the grade, not on the coefficient label. Let \(R\) exceed the maximum coordinate radius of their three stencils, including link endpoints. This also bounds the stencil of every grade-\(D\) counterterm, regardless of its coefficient size. The centered chamber at cutoff \(N\) has physical side \(a_{p_0}\), with its lattice endpoints rounded by at most \(a_N\). Choose the birth threshold so that \(4 r_m \leqslant a_{p_0} / 8\). For each such \(m\), choose the cutoff tail so that \(a_N (R + 2) < a_{p_0} / 8\). A collar anchor satisfies \(|y|_{\infty} < 4 r_m\), so its translated stencil lies strictly within \((- a_{p_0} / 4, a_{p_0} / 4)^4\); the stencils based at zero obey the same bound. This cube lies in the centered chamber even after rounding its endpoints. Thus all three readouts in the shell-node chamber receipt are chamber-interior. Gauge invariance, measurability and boundedness are inherited from the local-section polynomial on the compact lattice configuration space, while dependence only on chamber links is exactly the stencil inclusion just proved. The birth threshold is independent of the anchor and of the grade-\(D\) label. For each fixed cover anchor, the period tail places it at its intended site; this tail may depend on the anchor, as allowed by the shell-node proposition. The cutoff stencil tail is uniform over all coefficients, so the late event is common to every grade-\(D\) counterterm. This proves the required uniform-in-anchor chamber receipt from locality and lightness.

For completeness, the quota bound is an explicit finite product. Let \(r_D\) be the reference level and let \(v_j\) be the coefficient of the actual grade-\(D\) insertion transported to level \(j\). Calibration gives \(v_{r_D} = z\) and the adjacent column bound gives

\(\displaystyle \text{cs}_D (v_j) \leqslant C_{j + 1} L_j^D \text{cs}_D (v_{j + 1}), \qquad j \geqslant r_D,\)

where \(C_{j + 1}\) is the selected column budget, independent of cutoff and period. Norm equivalence at the fixed reference frame gives \(\|z\|_D \leqslant c_D \text{cs}_D (z)\). Iterating only from \(r_D\) to the fixed birth level \(m\) yields

\(\displaystyle \|z\|_D \leqslant c_D \prod_{j = r_D}^{m - 1} (C_{j + 1} L_j^D) \hspace{0.17em} \mathfrak{q}_{D, m, N, q} (z) .\)

The product is finite, positive and independent of \(N, q\). It supplies \(Q_m^{\text{quota}}\) in (17.44); no bound uniform as \(m \to \infty\) is asserted or needed.

17.4.4Scope of the shell construction

The menu and transfer receipts invoked above are the common-trajectory constructions of Sections 3.13, 3.12.4, 3.16, and 7.8; the selected public service and its endpoint support exponents are supplied by Theorem 4.15. The present argument supplies the remaining shell-specific composition: the two transported channel responses, finite mask and transfer record steps, coefficient ledger, placement, and collar norm/root bounds. The collar's late event is pointwise in its fixed anchor.

17.5The annular Ward field for the selected stress tensor

This section proves the shrinking-annulus implication used by the public stress-tensor route. Given the selected shell-node receipt, a finite linear combination of scale-covariant insertions that annihilates every field at one grade has vanishing flux on shrinking windows. Finite-dimensional linear algebra then represents those fluxes by one field at that grade. The production of the shell-node receipt is a separate quantitative obligation; it is not proved by the conditional argument below.

Write \(a_i\) for the physical spacing at level \(i\) and \(\lambda_i = \log (a_i^{- 1}) \to \infty\) for its logarithmic scale; thus \(a_i = e^{- \lambda_i}\). Write \(B_i\) for its support radius, and

\(\displaystyle r_m = 128 B_{m + 1}^2 a_{m + 1} .\)

The scale note used below says \(r_m \to 0\). Let \(\mathcal{F}_d\) denote the complex field space at grade \(d\). Write \(E_d\) for the finite-dimensional reference label space used at finite cutoff, and \(\pi_d : E_d \to \mathcal{F}_d\) for the selected table's quotient map. Finite-volume insertions take labels in \(E_d\); continuum testers take fields in \(\mathcal{F}_d\). Let \(\tau_{I, D} : \mathcal{F}_D \to \mathbb{C}\) be the tester associated with a watch \(I\). We write

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{A}_r = \{y \in \mathbb{R}^4 : r / 4 < |y| < 2 r\}, \qquad \mathcal{C}_r = \{y \in \mathbb{R}^4 : r / 16 < |y| < 4 r\} .\)

The wider collar \(\mathcal{C}_r\) is where the finite-volume shell correction is constructed; the desired annihilation is on \(\mathcal{A}_r\).

Concretely, \(\tau_{I, D} (z)\) is the insertion functional obtained by placing the grade-\(D\) field \(z\) at the distinguished point of \(I\) and retaining its other points and color labels as spectators. The flux functional is the window integral of the stress insertion against the window gradient and the Killing field:

\(\displaystyle \text{Flux}_J (\chi, U, I, v) = - \int_{\mathbb{R}^4} \sum_{\mu, \nu = 1}^4 \partial_{\mu} \chi (x) \hspace{0.17em} U_{\nu} (x - x_I) \hspace{0.17em} (I) \hspace{-0.17em} \text{asked} (J_{\mu \nu}, v) (x) \hspace{0.17em} dx,\)

where \(x_I\) is the distinguished point of the watch; all fluxes are thus written in coordinates centered at that point. The sign is fixed by the flux convention in the stress conservation law. All insertions and integrals here are the selected-table quantities; the shell argument uses their stated continuity and local conservation properties. The approximating counterterms below are the finite-volume objects.

For a finite-volume cell system \(\mathcal{T}\) and coupling \(\beta\), the root-test of a configuration observable \(F\) is

\(\displaystyle \text{RT}_{\beta, \mathcal{T}} (F) = \left| \mathbb{E}_{\beta} \prod_{u \in \mathcal{T}} F_u \right|^{1 / |\mathcal{T}|},\)

where \(F_u\) is the prescribed translate of \(F\) at cell \(u\), with reflection conjugation when that cell is reflected. The label norm \(\|z\|_D\) below is the fixed norm of the reference label space \(E_D\).

For the exact shell costs, put

\(\displaystyle c_m = e^{\lambda_m / 32}, \quad S (p_0, k) = \exp \hspace{-0.17em} \left( (a_{p_0} / a_k)^4 \hspace{0.17em} \textsf{T}_k + 5 \right), \quad X_m = \exp \hspace{-0.17em} \sqrt{\hat{\beta}_{m + 1}},\)

where \(\textsf{T}_k\) is the treasury quantity at level \(k\). Let \(V_m > 0\) be the product of the two selected channel payments at levels \((j_m, m + 1)\), and let \(n_m\) be the number of columns at level \(m\). The counterterm budget is

\(\displaystyle e_m = 32 (1 + n_m) a_{m + 1}^D X_m / V_m .\)

The channel payments, and hence \(V_m\) and \(e_m\), depend on the coefficient bounds of the two inserted labels. For each fixed birth level \(m\), the quota conversion supplies a finite factor \(Q_m^{\text{quota}} \geqslant 0\), so the producer bounds the correction label's coefficient norm by \(2 Q_m^{\text{quota}} e_m\). This bound is uniform in volume, period and collar point at that \(m\); no bound uniform in \(m\) is required, and the definition below asks only for some finite bound of this kind. The root-test cost, in contrast, is the decaying quantity \(2 S (p_0, k) c_m^{- 1}\).

17.5.1The shell-node hypothesis

Fix grades \(d, D, h \in \mathbb{N}\) with

\(\displaystyle D + h = d + 4.\) (17.46)

We name the exact shell-node input consumed below. This definition does not assert that the receipt has been produced from the public history.

Definition 17.19. (Shell-node receipt) For fixed grades \(d, D, h\) satisfying (17.46), a fixed family and selected history have the shell-node receipt if there are \(t > 1\) and levels \(j_m\), chosen before the opened levels, labels, or extraction order, such that

\(\displaystyle j_m \longrightarrow \infty, \qquad \frac{\lambda_{j_m}}{\lambda_m} \longrightarrow t,\)

and the scale note has the consequence

\(\displaystyle \frac{\log r_m}{\lambda_m} \longrightarrow - 1.\)

For every fixed opened reference level \(p_0\) and level \(k\) with \(p_0\) open at \(k\) and with \(k\) at least the reference threshold for grade \(D\), every \(v \in E_d\), \(T \in E_4\), and extraction order \(\mathfrak{f}\), there is \(m_0\) such that for every \(m \geqslant m_0\) and every fixed integer lattice anchor \(x \in \text{Cover} =\mathbb{Z}^4\) whose continuum location at level \(j_m\) lies in \(\mathcal{C}_{r_m}\), there is a label sequence \(z_x : \mathbb{N} \times \mathbb{N} \to E_D\) for which \(\mathfrak{f}\)-almost every \((N, q)\) has \(p_0 \leqslant N\) and

\(\displaystyle \|z_x (N, q)\|_D \leqslant A_m, \qquad \text{RT}_{\beta, \mathcal{T}} (\text{fragment} (z_x (N, q))) \leqslant A'_m := 2 S (p_0, k) c_m^{- 1} .\)

Here \(A_m < \infty\) is a constant which may depend on \(m\), the opening, the labels and the extraction order, but not on the anchor \(x\) or on \((N, q)\). The late filter set and sequence may depend on \(x\); the numeric coefficient and root-test bounds do not. The producer in Proposition 17.18 gives \(A_m = 2 Q_m^{\text{quota}} e_m\). The finite extraction order may encode any fixed finite family of watches; the receipt is available for every such family, with its tester costs fixed before the eventual volume/period choice. The associated finite-volume fragment is the product of the grade-four insertion at the collar point and the grade-\(d\) insertion at the selected point, multiplied by \(r_m^h\), minus the grade-\(D\) insertion \(z_x\) at the selected point. It lies in the prescribed chamber and has root-test norm bounded by the displayed cost. No common late filter over the entire (growing) set of collar grid points is asserted.

Here “eventually” is on the extraction filter over the volume and period variables, not a claim at each finite volume. Under its stated inputs, the construction in Proposition 17.18 has two stages. First, a two-channel counterterm construction builds the fragment. At the collar site \(x\), its two factors are the base insertion and the rescaled grade-four insertion; their product is corrected by one grade-\(D\) label. The grade identity (17.46) is exactly what makes that correction have the requested degree. The paid two-component insertion bounds its root-test cost, while the independent quota estimate bounds the correction label. Second, the corner-to-shell argument supplies common numeric bounds for all collar anchors. The late extraction event remains pointwise in the anchor; for a fixed interpolation stencil, one obtains a common event by intersecting the finitely many pointwise late events. This does not assert one event for the entire growing collar.

That construction uses the following numerical gates for this input. The economics choice is

\(\displaystyle t = 1 + \frac{1}{64 (d + 12)},\)

with a subsequence \(j_m\) for which \(\lambda_{j_m} / \lambda_m \to t\). The bound estimate supplies, eventually, positivity and a small two-channel payment budget, together with the grade-\(D\) correction-estimate. In this normalization its exponential denominator is \(c_m = \exp (\lambda_m / 32)\); at fixed opened \(p_0, k\), \(S (p_0, k)\) is fixed, so the root cost tends to zero. The separate lightness estimate supplies both

\(\displaystyle 32 r_m \leqslant a_{p_0}, \qquad a_{m + 1} (B_{m + 1}^2 + 1 + 2 \cdot 1024 B_{m + 1}^2) \leqslant a_{p_0}\)

eventually. These are used, respectively, for the collar/spacing margin and for the number of required coordinates in the opened chamber. The corner lemma combines them with the common-trajectory run, throw, root-room, and subsidized-payment bounds. Proposition 17.18 supplies this shell-node input, in the form of Definition 17.19, from the stated same-history menu, transfer, quota and bound receipts and the chamber receipt whose birth-level threshold is uniform over all anchors in each level-\(m\) collar. Its late extraction event may still depend on the fixed anchor; the scale note is proved in the paragraph following that proposition. The coefficient bound in that proposition has exactly the birth-level dependence allowed here. Their application on the selected family is given in Corollary 17.24 below.

The implication below takes the defined shell-node receipt as an input on one fixed family. It does not infer that receipt from the public extractor's record or change family, history, or scale sequence between the receipt and the stress law.

Openings.
A reference level \(m\) is opened at a level \(k\) (with parameter one) if \(m \leqslant k\) and \(B_k^2 \leqslant a_m / a_k\), as in Subsection 4.4. The receipt in Definition 17.19 is stated for every opened pair, so it says nothing unless openings exist. They do, under the selected tab schedule.

Lemma 17.20. (Every reference level is opened) Assume the tab-schedule bounds \(L_j = a_j / a_{j + 1} \geqslant 3\) and \(B_{j + 1}^2 \leqslant 2 B_j^2\) for every \(j\), where \(B_j\) is the radius in the definition of \(r_m\). If \(m\) is opened at \(k\), then it is opened at every \(k' \geqslant k\). For every level \(m\) and every \(k_1 \in \mathbb{N}\) there is \(k \geqslant k_1\) at which \(m\) is opened.

Proof. Persistence is the induction in the proof of Lemma 4.9: if \(B_j^2 \leqslant a_m / a_j\), then \(B_{j + 1}^2 \leqslant 2 B_j^2 \leqslant 2 a_m / a_j \leqslant L_j a_m / a_j = a_m / a_{j + 1}\). For existence put \(f_j = a_j B_j^2 > 0\). The two schedule bounds give

\(\displaystyle f_{j + 1} = a_{j + 1} B_{j + 1}^2 \leqslant \frac{a_j}{L_j} \hspace{0.17em} 2 B_j^2 \leqslant \frac{2}{3} f_j,\)

so \(f_j \leqslant (2 / 3)^{j - m} f_m \to 0\) as \(j \to \infty\). Choose \(k_0 \geqslant m\) with \(f_{k_0} \leqslant a_m\), that is \(B_{k_0}^2 \leqslant a_m / a_{k_0}\); then \(m\) is opened at \(k_0\). By persistence it is opened at \(k = \max (k_0, k_1)\).\(\Box\)

A slotwise bound before the physical Ward construction.
The derivative bound needed by the annulus argument can be obtained without analytic continuation or physical rotations. Retain the centered one-field service and the all-copy construction of Subsection 5.3. For a fixed list of labels \(v_1, \ldots, v_n\) of grades \(d_1, \ldots, d_n\), put \(A = \sum_{i = 1}^n (d_i + 1)\). We claim that for every \(\alpha > 1\) there is \(C_{\alpha, \boldsymbol{v}}\) such that the slab operator of this word, with mutual and boundary separation at least \(r \in (0, 1]\), satisfies
\(\displaystyle \|A_d (w)\| \leqslant C_{\alpha, \boldsymbol{v}} r^{- \alpha^2 A} .\) (17.47)

Constants may depend on the fixed word of labels, but not on its positions or the slab width. First the adjacent-increment estimate above gives \(a_j \geqslant a_{j - 1}^{\alpha}\) on a tail. If \(j \geqslant F\) is the first level with \(a_j < \epsilon\), then

\(\displaystyle c_{\alpha} \epsilon^{\alpha} \leqslant a_j < \epsilon, \qquad c_{\alpha} = \min (1, a_F) > 0.\)

For \(j > F\) this follows from \(a_{j - 1} \geqslant \epsilon\); for \(j = F\) it follows from \(\epsilon \leqslant 1\). Enlarge \(F\) to cover all fixed labels, their signed reflections and adjoints. Centered service with slack one bounds the bound of slot \(i\) at level \(k\) by \(J_i a_k^{- (d_i + 1)}\). It is important to retain each \(d_i\), rather than replace all of them by their maximum.

Choose \(m\) first with \(a_m < r / 32\). The lower bound just proved gives \(a_m \geqslant br^{\alpha}\), with \(b = c_{\alpha} 32^{- \alpha} > 0\). Choose \(0 < u < \min (1 / 32, b / 21)\), and then choose \(k\) first with \(a_k < u (r / n)^{\alpha}\). Since \(n \geqslant 1\) and \(\alpha > 1\),

\(\displaystyle 20 na_k < 20 ur^{\alpha} n^{1 - \alpha} < br^{\alpha} \leqslant a_m, \qquad a_k \geqslant c_{\alpha} u^{\alpha} (r / n)^{\alpha^2} .\)

Thus the two-scale placement argument of the all-copy proof applies unchanged. Its mixed-cell bound holds with different bounds in the main slots: apply the planted inequality without replacing the individual root bounds by a common maximum. In the reflected pairing of \(2 q\) main copies, each slot appears \(4 q\) times, so its bound is

\(\displaystyle X^{2 |v|} \left( \prod_{i = 1}^n J_i a_k^{- (d_i + 1)} \right)^{4 q} .\)

The exterior bound \(X\) remains independent of the replica number \(q\), exactly as in the existing proof. The kernel history-bound lemma therefore gives operator norm at most the product in parentheses. The lower bound for \(a_k\) proves (17.47), absorbing the fixed \(n\) and the fixed slot bounds in \(C_{\alpha, \boldsymbol{v}}\). Padding to the original slab width is a contraction. Uniqueness on the dense positive word domain identifies this operator with the already constructed operator, so obtaining a sharper estimate makes no new theory choice.

Compression and evaluation at a character are contractions. Translating into a slab with fixed positive boundary padding therefore gives the same bound for the selected correlations at all configurations with mutual sup separation at least \(r\). This is the proof of Proposition 5.20, now retaining the slotwise exponents. The exact slot derivative identity raises only the differentiated slot's grade, one per derivative. Thus for every fixed integer \(Q\), all derivatives of orders \(0 \leqslant n \leqslant Q\) in the moving slot satisfy

\(\displaystyle | \partial^n S_{\boldsymbol{v}} (x) | \leqslant C_{Q, \alpha} r^{- \alpha^2 (A + n)} .\)

The finitely many derivative labels and coordinate directions permit one maximum of the constants and thresholds. Choose \(\alpha > 1\) so close to one that \((\alpha^2 - 1) (A + Q) \leqslant 1\). For \(0 < r \leqslant 1\) this gives

\(\displaystyle | \partial^n S_{\boldsymbol{v}} (x) | \leqslant C_Q r^{- (A + n + 1)}, \qquad 0 \leqslant n \leqslant Q.\) (17.48)

The exponent has slope one in the derivative order. Choosing \(\alpha\) after the finite derivative request changes only proof constants and thresholds, not the selected schedule, labels or correlation table.

For each fixed watch, the stress slot has grade four and the other slots include its distinguished field and spectators. At a collar point \(y\) close to the distinguished origin, the least separation of the full word is at least \(c |y|\) for a fixed \(c > 0\): the remaining points are fixed and separated, and the only shrinking separation is between the stress slot and the origin. Hence (17.48) yields \(|H_{a, n, m} (y) | \leqslant C_a |y|^{- (A_a + n + 1)}\) uniformly over the collar. For finitely many watches choose \(Q\) so large that \(Q (t - 1) > \max_a (A_a + 1)\). Then

\(\displaystyle a_{j_m}^Q r_m^{- (A_a + Q + 1)} \longrightarrow 0,\)

because its logarithm divided by \(s_m\) tends to \(- Qt + (A_a + Q + 1) < 0\). This is exactly the remainder needed by the finite-order interpolation in the annular-field argument. Equivalently one may use the cloud allowance \(c_a = \sum_{\text{spectator slots}} d_i + (\text{number of spectator slots})\); then \(4 + n + 2 + c_a = A_a + n + 1\).

17.5.2Vanishing against a finite tester annihilator

Lemma 17.21. (shrinking-annulus annihilation) Let \(S\) be the selected hierarchy, \(V\) a derivative plan for \(S\), and \(J\) a stress conserved with respect to \(V\) and the spin action of \(S\). Assume the lattice configurations used in the shell argument are dense in the separated configuration space and that the flux and tester functionals are continuous there. Retain the centered-cell spectator bounds and the ordered moving-label point-limit identities on this same hierarchy and extraction. Assume the tab-schedule bounds of Lemma 17.20. For the finite family of watches \(I_1, \ldots, I_s\) below, let \(p_0\) be the largest of their reference depths, with \(p_0 = 0\) when \(s = 0\), and fix a level \(k \geqslant \text{ref} (D)\) at which \(p_0\) is opened; such a \(k\) exists by Lemma 17.20. Both \(p_0\) and \(k\) depend on the family. Take \(v \in \mathcal{F}_d\), and let \(U : \mathbb{R}^4 \to \mathbb{R}^4\) be a smooth homogeneous Killing field of degree \(p \in \mathbb{N}\), so \(U (ry) = r^p U (y)\) for \(r > 0\). Suppose both

\(\displaystyle D + (3 + p) = d + 4\) (17.49)

and (17.46) hold, and the shell-node receipt holds at \((p_0, k)\) for the finite family of watches \(I_1, \ldots, I_s\). Fix its witnessing scale ratio \(t > 1\). For each watch \(I_a\), the moving insertion is a component of the grade-four stress, so its initial tag degree is \(q_a = 4\); let \(c_a\) be the sum of grade plus one over all spectator slots, including the fixed grade-\(d\) insertion \(v\). The extra one per slot is an allowed enlargement of the spectator allowance and pays the centered-service slack. Choose an integer \(q_{\ast} \geqslant 1\) so large that

\(\displaystyle q_a + q_{\ast} + 2 + c_a < q_{\ast} t \quad (1 \leqslant a \leqslant s) .\)

For \(0 \leqslant n \leqslant q_{\ast}\), let \(H_{a, n, m} (y)\) denote the scalar insertion with the moving stress component of \(J\) at \(y\) raised \(n\) times, with the watch's fixed grade-\(d\) insertion \(v\) and all other spectator labels held fixed. The slotwise bound (17.48) supplies the quantitative separated-point bounds: for every sufficiently large \(m\) and every \(y \in \mathcal{C}_{r_m}\),

\(\displaystyle |H_{a, n, m} (y) | \leqslant C_a |y|^{- (q_a + n + 2 + c_a)}, \qquad 0 \leqslant n \leqslant q_{\ast},\)

where \(C_a\) is independent of \(m\) and \(y\). In physical coordinates,

\(\displaystyle \partial_{\ell} H_{a, n, m} = H_{a, n + 1, m} .\)

Thus along the lattice chord \(t \mapsto x + a_{j_m} te_{\ell}\) the derivative is \(a_{j_m} H_{a, n + 1, m}\); the \(n\)th chain member is \(a_{j_m}^n H_{a, n, m} (x + a_{j_m} te_{\ell})\). These explicit quantitative estimates, rather than smoothness alone, are the local regularity input used below. If \(g_1, \ldots, g_s \in \mathbb{C}\) satisfy

\(\displaystyle \sum_{a = 1}^s g_a \tau_{I_a, D} (z) = 0 \quad \text{for every } z \in \mathcal{F}_D,\) (17.50)

then, for any smooth windows \(\chi_a\) valid at the respective watch configurations,

\(\displaystyle \sum_{a = 1}^s g_a \hspace{0.17em} \text{Flux}_J (\chi_a, U, I_a, v) = 0.\) (17.51)

Proof. If \(s = 0\), the asserted sum is empty and equals zero. Hence assume \(s \geqslant 1\). Choose fixed representatives in \(E_d\) for \(v\) and in \(E_4\) for the sixteen components of \(J\). The quotient map sends these representatives to the given fields, and the shell receipt applies to these fixed labels. Choose a smooth radial bump \(\varphi\) on \(\mathbb{R}^4\) which equals one on the ball of radius \(1 / 2\) and vanishes outside the unit ball. The selected watch configurations have their distinguished point at the origin and all other points separated from it. Since \(r_m \to 0\), for all sufficiently large \(m\) the rescaled bump \(\varphi_m (y) = \varphi (y / r_m)\) is a valid window at every watch in the finite family, and no other insertion lies in its support. Conservation and the window-change identity therefore let us compute each flux using this same shrinking window.

For each pair of tensor indices \(i, j\), define

\(\displaystyle W_{ij} (y) = \partial_i \varphi (y) \hspace{0.17em} U_j (y), \qquad G_{ij, m} (y) = W_{ij} (y) \hspace{0.17em} r_m^{3 + p} \sum_{a = 1}^s g_a \hspace{0.17em} (I_a) \hspace{-0.17em} \text{asked} (J_{ij}, v) (r_m y) .\)

Each \(W_{ij}\) is integrable and supported in the unit ball. Its derivative vanishes near the origin, and the bump-support calculation places every point where \(W_{ij}\) is nonzero in the annulus with radii between \(r_m / 4\) and \(2 r_m\) after scaling. The annihilation argument below uses the shell estimate on the slightly wider collar, so endpoint conventions do not matter.

For each \(m\), the shell-node receipt has root-test tolerance

\(\displaystyle \epsilon_m = 2 S (p_0, k) c_m^{- 1} \longrightarrow 0.\)

For this fixed \(m\) the interpolation stencil uses only finitely many grid points. Intersect the late filter sets supplied separately for those points; their finite intersection is still late. The corresponding finite tuple of correction labels lies in a bounded subset of a finite-dimensional product space, with \(m\) held fixed, so pass to one convergent subnet and take its coordinate limits. Its bound \(A_m\) need not stay bounded as \(m\) increases: the correction labels disappear by the annihilator identity before the limit \(m \to \infty\) is taken. This single extraction handles every stencil point and every watch in the finite family; no subsequence is chosen watch by watch. The centered-cell bound gives a constant \(A_a\) for the fixed spectators of watch \(I_a\), so their moment with each fragment is bounded by \(A_a \epsilon_m\) on the late set. The opening dominates the watch's reference depth, as required by this bound. Along the convergent subnet, the ordered moving-label point-limit identity sends the correction insertion with limiting reference label \(z\) to \(\tau_{I_a, D} (\pi_D z)\). It sends the other term to the rescaled product insertion. Passing the inequality to the limit therefore gives an error at most \(A_a \epsilon_m\). Summing with coefficients \(g_a\), the limiting grade-\(D\) terms cancel by (17.50); the remaining norm is at most \(\epsilon_m \sum_a |g_a | A_a\). Thus the grid error tends to zero. Only this fixed finite family is used; no uniform bound over all watches is asserted. The one-coordinate Taylor argument described below transfers this grid error to the annulus. The two grade relations give \(h = 3 + p\). Thus the expression multiplied by \(W_{ij}\) is precisely the scale-corrected insertion that occurs after changing variables \(x = r_m y\) in the flux integral: the window gradient contributes \(r_m^{- 1}\), the homogeneous field contributes \(r_m^p\), and Lebesgue measure contributes \(r_m^4\). Their product is \(r_m^{3 + p}\), and \(h = d + 4 - D = 3 + p\). Hence this is exactly the factor \(r_m^h\) in the finite-tester estimate; there is no additional scaling factor.

The absolute value of each rescaled component integral is bounded by the uniform shell error times \(\int |W_{ij} |\). There are only sixteen components, so summing over them still tends to zero. The change-of-variables identity for the flux gives

\(\displaystyle \sum_a g_a \text{Flux}_J (\varphi_m, U, I_a, v) = - \sum_{i, j} \int_{\mathbb{R}^4} G_{ij, m} (y) \hspace{0.17em} dy.\)

The left side is independent of \(m\) by conservation and the support separation. The right side tends to zero by the preceding integrable bound. Therefore that fixed left side is zero. Replacing \(\varphi_m\) by \(\chi_a\) uses the same window-change identity separately at each watch, proving (17.51).\(\Box\)

The grid-to-annulus interpolation has an elementary quantitative form. For \(q \geqslant 0\) and \(P > 0\), set

\(\displaystyle A_0 (P) = 1, \qquad A_{q + 1} (P) = 1 + 4 PA_q (P) .\)

Suppose \(f_0, \ldots, f_q : [- P, P] \to \mathbb{C}\) are differentiable with \(f_i' = f_{i + 1}\) for \(i < q\), \(|f_q | \leqslant \epsilon\) throughout the interval, and \(|f_0 (x_j) | \leqslant \epsilon\) at \(2^q\) ordered points whose consecutive gaps are at least one. Then

\(\displaystyle \sup_{[- P, P]} |f_0 | \leqslant 2 A_q (P) \epsilon .\) (17.52)

For the proof first take all functions real-valued. Pair consecutive nodes. The real mean-value theorem supplies an intermediate point at which \(f_1\) is the difference quotient; its absolute value is at most \(2 \epsilon\) because the gap is at least one. The \(2^{q - 1}\) intermediate points remain ordered with gaps at least one. Induction applied to \(f_1, \ldots, f_q\) bounds \(|f_1 |\) by \(2 A_{q - 1} (P) \epsilon\). Integrating from one original node to an arbitrary point costs at most \(2 P\) times that bound, and the value at the node costs at most \(\epsilon\), giving \(A_q (P) \epsilon\). For complex functions apply this real estimate separately to real and imaginary parts. Their two bounds imply the stated complex estimate, since \(|z| \leqslant | \Re z| + | \Im z|\).

The common-limit argument applies separately at each of the finitely many grid nodes in the interpolation stencil. Its estimate is uniform in the node because the shell-node receipt and the watch-cost constants are uniform on the collar. At those nodes the nonzero-coordinate condition places every watch in the generic region required by its cost-limit estimate.

To pass from those grid points to an arbitrary point of the annulus, the derivative plan supplies the chains in (17.52). Along a coordinate line, differentiating a disc insertion raises its first tag by one and multiplies by the level spacing. Thus the \(i\)th chain member is the finite sum with first tag raised \(i\) times and prefactor \(a_{j_m}^i\), and its derivative is the next member. Since \(\lambda_{j_m} / \lambda_m \to t > 1\) while \(r_m\) has logarithmic spatial rate \(- 1\), the choice of \(q_{\ast}\) above gives

\(\displaystyle q_a + q_{\ast} + 2 + c_a < q_{\ast} t, \qquad \text{equivalently} \qquad q_{\ast} (t - 1) > q_a + 2 + c_a .\)

The iterated derivative estimate gives a uniform top-derivative bound of the form

\(\displaystyle Ca_{j_m}^{q_{\ast}} r_m^{- (q_a + q_{\ast} + 2 + c_a)},\)

where \(C\) is independent of \(m\). Its logarithm divided by \(\lambda_m\) tends to \(- q_{\ast} t + (q_a + q_{\ast} + 2 + c_a) < 0\), so the bound tends to zero. For the finite-node interpolation, induct over the set \(K\) of axes not yet discretized. On the next axis choose the nonzero shifted lattice coordinate provided by the one-dimensional stencil; leave the other coordinates fixed. At an intermediate stage only the already chosen coordinates are known to be nonzero. At the final stage all four are nonzero, so the grid-point representation and genericity estimates apply. Each coordinate move is at most \(r_m / 128\); therefore the total displacement is at most \(|K| r_m / 128 \leqslant r_m / 32\), within the collar margin. The collar-margin condition \(\text{arm} (q_{\ast}) a_{j_m} \leqslant r_m / 128\) keeps each segment in the wider collar. Applying (17.52) successively in the four coordinates gives a fixed finite factor, independent of \(m\). The grid error and the top derivative both tend to zero, proving uniform convergence on the annulus. The constants depend on the finite family, the grade, and the chosen chain length, but not on \(m\).

17.5.3One field represents every flux

Proposition 17.22. (annular flux representation) Assume, for every finite family of watches, the hypotheses of Lemma 17.21 for that family: the tab-schedule bounds, the shell-node receipt at the opened pair \((p_0, k)\) chosen for that family, and the separated-point bounds with their constants \(C_a\), cloud degrees \(c_a\) and chain length \(q_{\ast}\) for that family. Assume also that the hierarchy is faithful at grade \(D\): a field in \(\mathcal{F}_D\) whose testers \(\tau_{I, D}\) vanish for every watch \(I\) is zero. For each \(v \in \mathcal{F}_d\) and each smooth homogeneous Killing field \(U\) of degree \(p\), there exists one field \(b \in \mathcal{F}_D\) such that for every watch \(I\), every compatible smooth window \(\chi\), and every separated point configuration,

\(\displaystyle \text{Flux}_J (\chi, U, I, v) = \tau_{I, D} (b) .\)

The same \(b\) works for all configurations, windows, and watches.

Proof. For a finite family \(I_1, \ldots, I_s\), define linear maps

\(\displaystyle T : \mathcal{F}_D \to \mathbb{C}^s, \quad T (z) = (\tau_{I_1, D} (z), \ldots, \tau_{I_s, D} (z)), \qquad a = (\text{Flux}_J (\chi_1, U, I_1, v), \ldots) .\)

Lemma 17.21 says that every linear functional annihilating \(\text{range} \hspace{0.17em} T\) also annihilates \(a\). In finite-dimensional linear algebra, a subspace is the common kernel of all linear functionals vanishing on it. Hence \(a \in \text{range} \hspace{0.17em} T\) and there is \(b\) with \(T (b) = a\). This is the finite-tester step; it does not presume an infinite-dimensional Riesz representation.

Choose a finite faithful family of watches whose testers distinguish elements of \(\mathcal{F}_D\). Such a family exists because the complete family of watch evaluations separates fields, by the assumed faithfulness of the hierarchy at grade \(D\), and a finite-dimensional field space has a finite subfamily of any separating family that still separates. Apply the finite result to that family to obtain \(b\). For any further watch \(I\), apply the result to the union of \(I\) and the faithful family. The hypotheses of Lemma 17.21, assumed for every finite family, apply to this union, with its own opening \((p_0, k)\) chosen as in the lemma; the opening chosen for the faithful family alone may be too shallow for \(I\). The finite result gives \(b'\). On the faithful family, \(b'\) and \(b\) have the same tester values, so faithfulness implies \(b' = b\). Therefore the flux identity holds for \(I\) as well. This argument also handles every compatible window, since the window-change identity identifies its flux with the chosen window value. Finally, the lattice configurations used above are dense among separated configurations. Both flux and the tester/disc functional are continuous there, so the identity extends from those configurations to all separated point configurations.\(\Box\)

17.5.4Translation and rotation Ward maps

Apply Proposition 17.22 first to each constant Killing field \(U (y) = e_{\mu}\), of degree \(p = 0\), with output grade \(D = d + 1\). For every input grade \(d\) and direction \(\mu\) this gives a complex-linear map \(P_{d, \mu} : \mathcal{F}_d \to \mathcal{F}_{d + 1}\). Linearity follows because both flux and the finite faithful tester map are linear; uniqueness on the faithful family makes the representing field linear in the input. The translation identity identifies the front insertion of \(P_{d, \mu} v\) with the stress flux.

Next take the rotational Killing field \(U (y) = Ky\), where \(K\) is skew-symmetric. It is homogeneous of degree one, so the grade relation gives \(D = d\). The same construction yields maps \(R_{d, K} : \mathcal{F}_d \to \mathcal{F}_d\) which are complex-linear in the field and real-linear in \(K\), by linearity of the flux and uniqueness on the faithful testers. The angular-flux identity follows in coordinates centered at each watch's distinguished point, as in the flux definition. These two families are precisely the translation and rotation Ward maps; no additional stress tensor is selected in this step.

Remark 17.23. (public same-history application) The public route keeps the relevant objects on the family selected by the run: the extraction, hierarchy, finite-volume laws, and conserved stress all come from that same record. The endpoint invokes Proposition 17.18 on this family. It also needs the same ordered extraction, point-limit identities, centered-cell bounds, smooth faithful hierarchy and derivative plan used in the tester argument, the tab-schedule bounds that give an opening above the depths of each finite family of watches (Lemma 17.20), and the two schedule properties behind the scale note. The shell receipt alone supplies none of these. The resulting Ward conclusion is conditional on these inputs and on the incoming hypotheses of that proposition.

The argument proves finite-tester annihilation and the unique-field representation conditional on the shell-node receipt and the stated regularity/conservation data. The local producer of that receipt is Proposition 17.18; its same-history analytic and service inputs remain explicit dependencies. These quantitative hypotheses must hold for the selected history.

Corollary 17.24. (Annular responses on the selected family) On the common funded family, the centered conserved stress constructed in Subsection 17.3 has translation and rotation response fields with the flux identities of Proposition 17.22, on its same extracted smooth faithful hierarchy and derivative plan.

Proof. The service, terminal keeper and same-interface responses are furnished by Theorem 4.15; their selected schedule and reserve conditions are verified in Lemma 16.31. The shell-specific finite-mask inequalities and whole-polynomial transfer are checked in the actual fine finite-mask payment. The height growth is Lemma 17.17, with the retained adjacent-stage delay just verified. The shell schedule, lightness, uniformly placed stencils and finite quota product are proved in the selected-family placement argument. Thus Proposition 17.18 applies with this very family and its fixed stress representatives. Its coefficient bound depends on the birth level, as permitted by Definition 17.19; no uniform bound over birth levels is required.

Fix a finite family of watches. Choose a reference depth beyond their finitely many centered-cell depths. The selected lightness estimates imply that this depth is opened at every sufficiently large subsequent level: on a tail, \(a_{j + 1} B_{j + 1}^2 \leqslant (2 / 3) a_j B_j^2\), so the latter quantity tends to zero. Choose one opening for this watch family and then apply the shell-node receipt there. At each fixed interpolation stencil the intersection of its finitely many late sets is late. This is the only simultaneous use of collar anchors; it does not impose one late event on the entire growing collar.

The point limits and smooth representatives are those of the original extracted table. Their finite differences represent the derivative plan by Subsection 16.11; the all-spectator kernel identification of Subsection 16.12 ensures that passing to the field quotient changes none of those tested limits. The centered estimates survive that fixed extraction. The slotwise all-copy estimate (17.47) and its consequence (17.48) give the separated-point derivative bounds used in annular interpolation, with constants depending on the fixed family of watches. Conservation is that of the selected stress and holds on the whole separated domain. Since \(a_{j_m} \to 0\), lattice approximants are dense in every fixed separated configuration window. Smoothness of the hierarchy and the compact support of the shell gradient make the testers and shell fluxes continuous there, so the identities extend from these approximants. Faithfulness is the defining all-spectator quotient property. These verify the remaining hypotheses of Lemma 17.21 for this family.

The family was arbitrary. Proposition 17.22 therefore represents each flux by one field, independent of watches and windows. Constant Killing fields give translations and linear skew Killing fields give rotations, with the grades prescribed by (17.46). Linearity of their fluxes and faithfulness give linearity of the response maps. All operations use the same stress and extraction, completing the claimed application.\(\Box\)

17.6Positive spectral estimates for the exterior packets

The exterior Ward argument uses spectral estimates before full rotation covariance has been proved. The spectral measure for one coordinate axis therefore cannot be identified with the spectral measure for another axis. The link between them will instead be the same tested correlation, with its reflection sign. We develop that link and the estimates it gives.

Write \(\epsilon_n = e^{- n}\). A sequence has exponent \(\alpha\) if its norm is at most \(Ce^{\alpha n}\) for all sufficiently large \(n\), for some fixed \(C\). Constants and the starting index may depend on the fixed fields and test profiles. Finite sums preserve an exponent; products add exponents; and an estimate with exponent \(\alpha\) also holds with any larger exponent. These rules follow directly from the triangle and product inequalities.

A cluster below means a finite complex linear combination of field words at fixed, internally distinct positions. A cluster is on the \(i\)-hyperplane if every point in every word has \(i\)th coordinate zero. It has reflection sign \(\eta_i \in \{1, - 1\}\) if reflection of its points and labels in that coordinate gives \(\eta_i\) times the cluster. For each axis \(i\), use its reflection-positive chamber and its joint translation spectral measure, as in the heat-vector construction. Denote the positive measure belonging to the cluster \(A\) by \(\mu_i^A\) and write \(E = p_i\). It is supported on \(E \geqslant 0\). The positive-time weight makes it locally finite in energy, including on \(0 \leqslant E \leqslant 1\); the measure itself need not have finite total mass.

For completeness, this measure is obtained without an arbitrary boundary extension. Let \(v_u\) be the chamber vector of \(A\) translated by \(u\) into the positive half-space, and let \(\rho_u\) be its finite joint spectral measure. The commuting real-time group supplied by Subsection 6.1, together with the three spatial translation groups, has the joint measure of Subsection 6.10; only its Euclidean application is used here. Set \(d \mu_i^A = e^{2 uE} d \rho_u\). For \(v > u\), the semigroup relation \(v_v = e^{- (v - u) H_i} v_u\) gives \(d \rho_v = e^{- 2 (v - u) E} d \rho_u\), so the definition is independent of \(u\). Two admissible placements are compared through their maximum. On \(E \leqslant 1\) the multiplier is at most \(e^{2 u}\), proving the asserted finite mass there.

The spectral input used here has the following precise form. If all the points of \(A\) lie above \(- u\) in coordinate \(i\), then, for each \(j \ne i\) and \(k \geqslant 0\),

\(\displaystyle \int e^{- 2 uE} |p_j |^{2 k} \hspace{0.17em} d \mu_i^A (p) < \infty\)

when the inequalities at \(- u\) are strict. If \(A\) is on the \(i\)-hyperplane, there is, for each \(j, k\), an integer \(D\) such that

\(\displaystyle \int (1 + E)^{- D} |p_j |^{2 k} \hspace{0.17em} d \mu_i^A (p) < \infty .\) (17.53)

These assertions use only positive-time evolution, spatial translations, and the polynomial separated-point derivative bounds of the hierarchy. Here is the passage from those bounds to the second assertion. The Gram function of the translated positive-time cluster is a finite sum of separated correlations. Its \(2 k\)th transverse derivative is bounded by \(Ct^{- D_0}\) when the reflected halves have distance \(t \in (0, 1]\). The positive spectral representation gives

\(\displaystyle \int e^{- tE} |p_j |^{2 k} \hspace{0.17em} d \mu_i^A \leqslant Ct^{- D_0} .\)

To justify the moment before differentiating under the integral, take the \(k\)th forward difference of the translated vector. Its squared norm is the integral of \(|e^{ihp_j} - 1|^{2 k} / |h|^{2 k}\) against the damped measure. The iterated fundamental theorem of calculus bounds this squared norm by the corresponding \(2 k\)th Gram derivative. Fatou gives the asserted moment bound. Dominated convergence, using \(|e^{ix} - 1| \leqslant |x|\), then gives the moment identity. Finally integrate the preceding bound against \(t^{D_0} \hspace{0.17em} dt\) on \((0, 1)\). For \(E \geqslant 0\),

\(\displaystyle \int_0^1 t^{D_0} e^{- tE} \hspace{0.17em} dt \geqslant \frac{e^{- 1}}{D_0 + 1} (1 + E)^{- D_0 - 1} .\)

Tonelli proves (17.53). For a cluster with a strict positive-time margin, the same finite-difference argument at that fixed margin gives the exponentially weighted statement.

17.6.1Moment subtraction with a positive response

For a real smooth compactly supported profile \(f\), put \(f_s (x) = s^{- 1} f (x / s)\), \(s > 0\). Choose once an even nonnegative smooth function \(\varphi\), supported where \(1 \leqslant |x| \leqslant 2\), with integral one. Let \(L_j\), \(0 \leqslant j < m\), be the Lagrange polynomials for the nodes \((j + 2)^2\). Define a correction \(B_{m, s} f\) by

\(\displaystyle B_{m, s} f = \sum_{j < m} \sum_{k \geqslant 0} [X^k] L_j \hspace{0.17em} s^{- 2 k} \hspace{-0.17em} \left( \int x^{2 k} f (x) \hspace{0.17em} dx \right) (x \mapsto x^{- 2 k} \varphi (x))_{s (j + 2)} .\) (17.54)

The second sum is finite. All correction profiles vanish near zero. Interpolation of the monomials \(X^r\), \(r < m\), shows that \(R_{m, s} f = f - B_{m, s} f\) has zero moments of orders below \(2 m\) when \(f\) is even. In fact the even moment of order \(2 r\) of a term with index \(j, k\) is \((s (j + 2))^{2 r}\) times the corresponding moment of \(x^{- 2 k} \varphi\), multiplied by its coefficient. Summing the coefficients is exactly \(\sum_j L_j (X) (j + 2)^{2 r} = X^r\); the odd moments vanish by parity.

For an even real profile let \(\mathcal{L}f (E)\) be its temporal Laplace response in the spectral formula. With the Fourier normalization used there, for \(E > 0\) it is

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{L}f (E) = 2 E \int_{\mathbb{R}} \frac{f (x)}{x^2 + E^2} \hspace{0.17em} dx.\)

Set \(M_{m, s} f =\mathcal{L} (R_{m, s} f)\). The rational interpolation identity

\(\displaystyle \frac{1}{x + y} - \sum_{j < m} \frac{L_j (x)}{(j + 2)^2 + y} = \frac{1}{x + y} \prod_{j < m} \frac{(j + 2)^2 - x}{(j + 2)^2 + y}\)

follows by multiplying by \(x + y\): the two polynomials agree at the \(m\) nodes and at \(x = - y\). Scaling and integrating it gives

\(\displaystyle M_{m, s} f (E) = 2 E \int \hspace{-0.17em} \hspace{-0.17em} \int \frac{\varphi (v) f (x)}{x^2 + E^2} \prod_{j < m} \frac{(sv (j + 2))^2 - x^2}{(sv (j + 2))^2 + E^2}, dx \hspace{0.17em} dv.\) (17.55)

If \(f \geqslant 0\) and its support is in \([- s, s]\), every factor in this product lies between zero and one. The response is nonnegative. The identity and its bounds extend to \(E = 0\) by continuity of the Laplace response; this retains any spectral mass at zero.

For fixed \(m\) and \(D \leqslant 2 m\), the same product gives, whenever \(s \geqslant 1\) and the support of \(f_w\) lies in \([- s, s]\),

\(\displaystyle 0 \leqslant M_{m, s} f_w (E) \leqslant C_{m, f, D} (w^{- 1} + s^D) (1 + E)^{- D} .\) (17.56)

For \(E \leqslant 1\), bound \(f_w\) by \(w^{- 1} \|f\|_{\infty}\) and use \(\int (x^2 + E^2)^{- 1} dx = \pi / E\). For \(E \geqslant 1\), the product is at most \(C_m \min (1, (s / E)^{2 m})\). The remaining integral, after multiplication by \(2 E\), is bounded by \(2 \int f\) because \(E \geqslant 1\). These are the two terms in (17.56), with a larger fixed constant. Combining it with (17.53) gives

\(\displaystyle \int M_{m, s} f_w (E) |p_j |^{2 k} \hspace{0.17em} d \mu_i^A \leqslant C (w^{- 1} + s^D),\) (17.57)

where \(D\) is chosen first and \(m\) is then chosen sufficiently large. In particular \(D\) does not grow with the subsequently chosen subtraction order \(m\). The ordinary double integrals needed for the spectral representation are also absolutely integrable. Zero moments through order \(2 m - 1\) imply \(| \widehat{R_{m, s} f} (t) | \leqslant C |t|^{2 m}\) near zero by Taylor's formula; away from zero it is Schwartz. Thus its absolute Laplace integral is at most \(C' (1 + E)^{- 2 m - 1}\). Choose \(2 m + 1 \geqslant D\) and apply (17.53). This justifies Fubini before using reflection-sign cancellation, rather than inferring it from a signed integral.
